<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label{def:bulging}
    Let $F$ be a skew product as in \eqref{eq:skew-product-form}, and let $U\subset \mathbb{C}$ be a Fatou component of $f$.
    We say that $U$ is
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item \emph{bulging} if $U \times \set{0} \subset \mathcal{F}(F)$, and
        \item \emph{non-bulging} if $U \times \set{0} \not\subset \mathcal{F}(F)$.
    \end{enumerate} 
    If $U$ is non-bulging, we call it
    \begin{enumerate}
        \setcounter{enumi}{2}
        \item \emph{nowhere bulging} if $U \times \set{0} \subset \mathcal{J}(F)$, and
        \item \emph{partially bulging} if both $(U\times \set{0}) \cap \mathcal{J}(F) \neq \emptyset$ and $(U \times \set{0}) \cap \mathcal{F}(F) \neq \emptyset$. 
    \end{enumerate}
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:basin-inf-classification}
    Let $f \colon \mathbb{C} \to \mathbb{C}$ be a polynomial of degree $d \geq 2$, let $r \colon \mathbb{C}^2 \to \mathbb{C}$ be a polynomial in $z$ of degree $\ell$ vanishing on $\set{w=0}$, and let $g$ be a nonconstant entire function with an attracting fixed point at $0$, where $m \geq 1$ denotes the multiplicity of $g$ at $0$.
    Consider the skew product
    \begin{equation*}
        F(z,w) = (f(z) + r(z,w), g(w)).
    \end{equation*}
    Then the basin at infinity of $f$ is non-bulging for $F$ if and only if $m < d < \ell$.
    Moreover, if the basin is non-bulging, then it is nowhere bulging.
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:nowhere-bulging}
    Let $f$ be an entire function with an orbitally unbounded Fatou component $U \subset \mathbb{C}$.
    Furthermore, let $g$ be a nonconstant entire function with $g(0) = 0$.
    Then there exists an entire function $h$ such that $U \times \set{0}$ lies in the Julia set of the skew product $F(z,w) = (f(z) + wh(z), g(w))$.
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
\label{def:orbitally-unbounded}
    Let $f$ be an entire function and $U \subset \mathbb{C}$ a Fatou component of $f$.
    We call $U$ \emph{orbitally unbounded} if there exist $z \in U$ and a subsequence $(f^{n_k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of iterates such that $f^{n_k}(z) \to \infty$ as $k \to \infty$.
    Otherwise, we call $U$ \emph{orbitally bounded}.
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- definition-3: body_md -->
\label{def:absorbing-domain}
    Let $f$ be an entire function and $U \subset \mathbb{C}$ a Fatou component of $f$.
    A domain $V \subset U$ is called \emph{absorbing in $U$ for $f$} if $f(V) \subset V$ and if for every $z \in U$, there exists $n \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$ such that $f^n(z) \in V$.
<!-- end definition-3 -->
<!-- definition-4: body_md -->
Let $X$ be a complex manifold. 
    A family $ \mathcal{F} \subset \operatorname{Hol}(X, \mathbb{C}^2) $ of holomorphic maps is called \emph{normal} if every sequence in $\mathcal{F}$ has a subsequence that either converges uniformly on compact subsets to a limit in $\operatorname{Hol}(X, \mathbb{C}^2)$ or diverges uniformly to infinity on compact subsets.
<!-- end definition-4 -->
<!-- definition-5: body_md -->
\label{def:fatou-2d}
    Let $F\colon \mathbb{C}^2 \to \mathbb{C}^2 $ be a holomorphic map.
    The \emph{Fatou set} $\mathcal{F}(F)$ of $F$ is the set of points in $\mathbb{C}^2$ that have a neighborhood on which the family of iterates $(F^n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ is normal. 
    The \emph{Julia set} $\mathcal{J}(F)$ of $F$ is the complement $\mathbb{C}^2 \setminus \mathcal{F}(F)$.
<!-- end definition-5 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lemma:backward-invariance}
    For any holomorphic map $F\colon \mathbb{C}^2 \to \mathbb{C}^2$, the Fatou set is backward invariant, that is,
    \begin{equation*}
        F^{-1}(\mathcal{F}(F)) \subset \mathcal{F}(F).
    \end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:runge-1}
	Let $(E_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ be a sequence of compact subsets of $\mathbb{C}$ such that
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item the sets $\mathbb{C} \setminus E_n$ are connected for all $n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$,
		\item the sets are pairwise disjoint, i.e., $E_n \cap E_m = \emptyset$ for all $n\neq m$, and
		\item $\min\lvert E_n \rvert = \min\set{\lvert z \rvert \mid z \in E_n} \to \infty$ as $n \to \infty$.
	\end{enumerate}
	Suppose that $\psi$ is holomorphic in a neighborhood of $E = \bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}} E_n$, and let $(\varepsilon_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ be a sequence of positive real numbers. Then there exists an entire function $h$ such that
	\begin{equation*}
		\abs{h(z) - \psi(z)} < \varepsilon_n
	\end{equation*}
	for all $z \in E_n$ and $n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$.
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- definition-6: body_md -->
Let $G \subset \mathbb{C}$ be open and connected. A relatively closed subset $E \subset G$ is called a \emph{Weierstraß set} if every continuous function ${f \colon E \to \mathbb{C}}$ which is holomorphic on the interior $\mathring{E}$ can be uniformly approximated on $E$ by functions in $\operatorname{Hol}(G,\mathbb{C})$, i.e., for every $\varepsilon > 0$ there exists $g \in \operatorname{Hol}(G, \mathbb{C})$ such that
	\begin{equation*}
		\abs{f(z) - g(z)} < \varepsilon
	\end{equation*} 
	for all $z \in E$.
<!-- end definition-6 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{thm:arakelyan}
	Let $G \subset \mathbb{C}$ be open and connected. A relatively closed subset $E \subsetneq G$ is a Weierstraß set if and only if the following properties hold:
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item $\widehat{G}\setminus E$ is connected, and
		\item $\widehat{G}\setminus E$ is locally connected at $\infty$,
	\end{enumerate}
    where $\widehat{G}$ is the one-point compactification of $G$.
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- definition-7: body_md -->
Let $G \subset \mathbb{C}$ be open and connected. A relatively closed subset $E \subset G$ is called a \emph{Carleman set} if for every continuous function $f \colon E \to \mathbb{C}$ which is holomorphic on the interior $\mathring{E}$ and for every $\varepsilon \colon E \to (0,\infty)$ continuous, there exists $g \in \operatorname{Hol}(G, \mathbb{C})$ satisfying
	\begin{equation*}
		\abs{f(z) - g(z)} < \varepsilon(z)
	\end{equation*}
	for all $z \in E$.
<!-- end definition-7 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{thm:nersesjan}
	Let $G \subset \mathbb{C}$ be open and connected. A relatively closed subset $E \subsetneq G$ is a Carleman set if and only if the following properties hold:
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item $\widehat{G}\setminus E$ is connected,
		\item $\widehat{G}\setminus E$ is locally connected at $\infty$, and
		\item for every compact $K \subset G$ there exists a neighborhood $V \subset \widehat{G}$ of $\infty$ such that no component of the interior $\mathring{E}$ intersects both $K$ and $V$,
	\end{enumerate}
    where $\widehat{G}$ is the one-point compactification of $G$.
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
\label{thm:weierstrass}
    Let $G_1 \subset G_2 \subset \dots$ be an increasing sequence of domains in $\mathbb{C}$ and $(f_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ a sequence of holomorphic functions $f_n\colon G_n \to \mathbb{C}$. Let $G \coloneqq \bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}} G_n$ and assume that $(f_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ converges uniformly on any compact subset of $G$ to a limit function $f$. Then $f$ is holomorphic on $G$.
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- definition-8: body_md -->
\label{def:polynomial-in-z}
    A holomorphic function $r \colon \mathbb{C}^2 \to \mathbb{C}$ is called a \emph{polynomial in $z$ of degree $\ell$} if it can be written as
    \begin{equation*}
        r(z,w) = \sum_{j = 0}^{\ell} r_j(w) z^j
    \end{equation*}
    for entire functions $r_0, \dots, r_\ell$ with $r_\ell \not\equiv 0$.
    By convention, we call the constant zero function a polynomial in $z$ of degree $-\infty$.
<!-- end definition-8 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lemma:basin-inf-classification-oneway}
    Under the hypotheses of \Cref{thm:basin-inf-classification}, if $d \geq \ell$ or $m \geq d$, then the basin at infinity of $f$ is bulging.
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
In particular, the basin at infinity is bulging whenever $F$ is a \emph{regular polynomial map of degree $d \geq 2$}, i.e., the components of $F$ are polynomials of degree $d$ and the homogeneous part $F_{\text{hom}}$ of degree $d$ satisfies $F_{\text{hom}}^{-1}(0,0) = \set{(0,0)}$.
    In this case $\ell \leq d$, since the first component of $F$ has degree $d$.
    This can also be deduced more directly from the growth condition
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:regular-poly}
        \liminf_{\norm{(z,w)} \to \infty} \frac{\norm{F(z,w)}}{\norm{(z,w)}^d} > 0,
    \end{equation}
    which is equivalent to regularity (see \cite[p. 158]{Bedford2000}).
    Indeed, from \eqref{eq:regular-poly} it follows that there exists $R > 0$ large enough that for all $(z,w) \in \mathbb{C}^2$ with $\norm{(z,w)} \geq R$,
    \begin{equation*}
        \norm{F(z,w)} \geq 2 \norm{(z,w)}.
    \end{equation*}
    Consequently, all points in $\set{(z,w) \in \mathbb{C}^2 \mid \norm{(z,w)} \geq R}$ diverge uniformly to infinity.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
We assume throughout this proof that $\ell \geq 0$.
    Otherwise, $\ell = -\infty$, which implies $r \equiv 0$.
    Then $F$ is a product map and all Fatou components are bulging.

    Since $r$ vanishes on the fiber $\set{w = 0}$, there exists a holomorphic function $h \colon \mathbb{C}^2 \to \mathbb{C}$ such that $r(z,w) = wh(z,w)$ for all $(z,w) \in \mathbb{C}^2$.
    The functions $f$ and $h$ can be written as
    \begin{equation*}
        f(z) = \sum_{j=0}^d a_j z^j \text{ and } h(z,w) = \sum_{j = 0}^{\ell} h_j(w) z^j
    \end{equation*}
    with coefficients $a_0, \dots, a_d \in \mathbb{C}$ where $a_d \neq 0$, and entire functions $h_0, \dots, h_\ell$ where $h_\ell \not\equiv 0$.

    Up to rescaling in $z$, we may assume that $a_d = 1$.
    Up to a local one-dimensional holomorphic conjugacy in $w$, we may assume that near $w = 0$, that is, on a set $D(0,\delta_g)$ for $0 < \delta_g < 1$ sufficiently small, the function $g$ is of the form $g(w) = \lambda w$ for some $\lambda \in \mathbb{D} \setminus\set{0}$ if $m =1$ or of the form $g(w) = w^m$ if $m \geq 2$.
    From now on, we work on $\mathbb{C} \times D(0,\delta_g)$ with $g$ in this normal form.

    For $z \in \mathbb{C}\setminus\set{0}$ and $w \in D(0,\delta_g)$, the first coordinate function of $F$ can be written as
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-1}
        f(z) + wh(z,w) = z^d \paren*{ 1 + \sum_{j=0}^{d-1} a_j z^{j-d} + w \sum_{k = 0}^{\ell} h_k(w) z^{k - d} }.
    \end{equation}
    Define $A\coloneqq \sum_{j=0}^{d-1} \abs{a_j}$ and $B \coloneqq \sum_{j=0}^{\ell} \sup_{\abs{w} \leq \frac{\delta_g}{2}} \abs{h_j(w)} > 0$. 

    We start with the case $\deg(f) = d \geq \ell$.
    Since $d \geq \ell$, if we fix any $\rho \geq \max\set{4A, 4}$ and $0 < \delta < \min\set*{\frac{1}{4B}, \frac{\delta_g}{2}}$, then for all $z \in \overline{D(0,\rho)}^c$ and $w \in D(0,\delta)$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-2}
        \abs*{\sum_{j=0}^{d-1} a_j z^{j-d} + w \sum_{k = 0}^{\ell} h_k(w) z^{k - d}} \leq A \frac{1}{\abs{z}} + B \abs{w} < A \frac{1}{\rho} + B \delta < \frac{1}{2}.
    \end{equation}
    Then, from \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-1} and \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-2} together with the reverse triangle inequality, it follows that
    \begin{equation*}
        \abs{f(z) + wh(z,w)} \geq \abs{z}^d \paren*{1 - \frac{1}{2}} \geq \abs{z} \frac{\rho}{2} \geq \frac{4}{2} \abs{z} = 2 \abs{z}
    \end{equation*}
    for all $z \in \overline{D(0,\rho)}^c$ and $w \in D(0,\delta)$.
    Inductively, we obtain for the $n$th iterate $(z_n,w_n) = F^n(z,w)$ that
    \begin{equation*}
        \abs{z_n} \geq 2^n \abs{z} \geq 2^n \rho \to \infty
    \end{equation*}
    uniformly as $n \to \infty$ on $\overline{D(0,\rho)}^c \times D(0,\delta)$. 
    This implies that $\overline{D(0,\rho)}^c \times \set{0} \subset \mathcal{F}(F)$.
    Since $\overline{D(0,\rho)}^c$ is an absorbing domain for the basin at infinity, in the sense of \Cref{def:absorbing-domain}, the claim follows using the backward invariance of the Fatou set as established in \Cref{lemma:backward-invariance}.

    We now turn to the second case and assume that $m \geq d$. 
    In particular, $m \geq 2$, and thus, $g(w) = w^m$, as well as $w_n = g^{n}(w) = w^{m^n}$ for all $w \in D(0,\delta_g)$ and $n \in\mathbb{N}_{0}$. 
    Fix any $\rho > \max\set{4A, 2^{d+1}}$ and deduce, as before, that
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-3}
        \abs*{\sum_{j=0}^{d-1} a_j z^{j-d}} \leq A \frac{1}{\rho} \leq \frac{1}{4}
    \end{equation}
    for all $z \in \overline{D(0,\rho)}^c$. Fix any $R > \rho$ and consider the annulus $A(0;\rho,R) \coloneqq \set{ z \in \mathbb{C} \mid \rho < \abs{z} < R}$.
    Observe that for $w \in D\paren*{0,\frac{\delta_g}{2}}$ and $z \in \overline{D(0,\rho)}^c$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-4}
        \abs*{w \sum_{k = 0}^{\ell} h_k(w) z^{k - d}} \leq B \abs{w} \abs{z}^\ell.
    \end{equation}
    Let $ 0 < \delta = \delta(R) < \frac{\delta_g}{2} $ be small enough that $\delta\paren*{\paren*{\frac{3}{2}}^{d} R}^{\ell}< \min\set*{\frac{1}{4B}, 1}$.
    Then, for all $n \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$ and all $(z,w) \in A(0;\rho,R) \times D(0,\delta)$, it follows that
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-5}
        B\paren*{ \abs{w} \paren*{\paren*{\frac{3}{2}}^{d} \abs{z}}^{\ell}}^{m^n} < B \paren*{\min\set*{\frac{1}{4B}, 1}}^{m^n} \leq \frac{1}{4}.
    \end{equation}
    We prove by induction that for all $z \in A(0;\rho,R)$, $ w \in D(0,\delta)$, and $n \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$, we have
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-6}
        \paren*{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{d^{n+1} - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z}^{d^n} \leq \abs{z_n} \leq \paren*{\frac{3}{2}}^{\frac{d^{n+1} - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z}^{d^n}.
    \end{equation}
    Indeed, fix any $z \in A(0;\rho,R)$ and $w \in D(0,\delta)$.
    The case $n = 0$ is evident. 
    Assume that \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-6} holds for some $n \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$. 
    Then, the $n$th iterate $z_n$ of $(z,w)$ satisfies
    \begin{equation*}
        \begin{split}
            \abs{z_n} \leq \paren*{\frac{3}{2}}^{\frac{d^{n+1} - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z}^{d^n} &\leq \paren*{\frac{3}{2}}^{d^{n+1}} \abs{z}^{d^n} = \paren*{\paren*{\frac{3}{2}}^{d} \abs{z}}^{d^n}.
        \end{split}
    \end{equation*}
    Also observe that
    \begin{equation*}
        \abs{z_n} \geq \paren*{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{d^{n+1} - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z}^{d^n} > \paren*{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{d^{n+1} - 1}{d - 1}} \rho^{d^n} \geq \rho
    \end{equation*}
    for $n \geq 1$, while $\abs{z_0} = \abs{z} > \rho$ trivially.
    From \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-4}, \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-5}, and $d \leq m$, it follows that
    \begin{alignat*}{1}
        \abs*{w_n \sum_{j = 0}^{\ell} h_j(w_n) z_n^{j - d}} &\leq B \abs{w_n} \abs{z_n}^\ell \\
         
        &\leq B \abs{w}^{m^n}\paren*{\paren*{\frac{3}{2}}^{d} \abs{z}}^{d^n \ell} \\
         
        &\leq B\paren*{ \delta \paren*{\paren*{\frac{3}{2}}^{d} R}^{\ell}}^{m^n} < \frac{1}{4}.
    \end{alignat*}
    We can apply \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-3}, and as in the first case, it follows that
    \begin{equation*}
        \paren*{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{d^{n+2} - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z}^{d^{n+1}} \leq \frac{1}{2} \abs{z_n}^{d} \leq \abs{z_{n+1}} \leq \frac{3}{2} \abs{z_n}^d \leq \paren*{\frac{3}{2}}^{\frac{d^{n+2} - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z}^{d^{n+1}},
    \end{equation*}
    because $z_{n+1} = f(z_n) + w_nh(z_n,w_n)$.
    This completes the induction.  

    To finish the proof, observe that \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-6} implies locally uniform divergence to infinity in the $z$-coordinate on the neighborhood $A(0;\rho,R) \times D(0, \delta)$ of $A(0;\rho,R) \times \set{0}$, since, having chosen $\rho > 2^{d+1}$, we obtain
    \begin{equation*}
        \paren*{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{d^{n+1} - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z}^{d^n} \geq \paren*{\frac{1}{2}}^{d^{n+1}} \rho^{d^n} = \paren*{\frac{1}{2^d}\rho}^{d^n} > 2^{d^n} \to \infty
    \end{equation*}
    as $n \to \infty$.
    Furthermore, since $R > \rho$ was arbitrary, the absorbing domain $\overline{D(0,\rho)}^c$ of the basin at infinity satisfies $\overline{D(0,\rho)}^c \times \set{0} \subset \mathcal{F}(F)$, and hence, as in the first case, the basin at infinity is bulging.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lemma:basin-at-inf-2}
    Under the hypotheses of \Cref{thm:basin-inf-classification}, the basin at infinity is non-bulging if $ m < d < \ell $.
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
We assume that $1 \leq m < d < \ell$.

    There exist $0 < \delta_g < 1$ and a one-dimensional holomorphic conjugacy on $D(0,\delta_g)$ conjugating $g$ to $w \mapsto \lambda w$ for some $\lambda \in \mathbb{D} \setminus\set{0}$ if $m =1$ or to $ w\mapsto w^m$ if $m \geq 2$.
    From now on, we work on $\mathbb{C} \times D(0,\delta_g)$ with $g$ in this normal form.

    To prove that the basin at infinity of $f$ is non-bulging, we show that there exists $z_0$ in the basin at infinity of $f$ such that points arbitrarily close to $(z_0, 0)$ have orbits coming back close to the origin $(0,0)$.
    Since $0$ is an attracting fixed point of $g$, this is trivial for the $w$-coordinate.
    For the $z$-coordinate, we show that there exists $R > 0$ such that for every $z_0 \in D(0,R)^c$, every $\varepsilon > 0$, and every $M \in \mathbb{N}_{0} $, there exist $\widetilde{w} = \widetilde{w}(\varepsilon,M) \in D(0,\varepsilon)$ and $n = n(\varepsilon,M) \geq M$ with
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-7}
        \pi_z F^{n}(z_0,\widetilde{w}) = 0.
    \end{equation}
    This is sufficient to show non-bulging since it prevents uniform divergence to infinity on any neighborhood of $(z_0,0)$.

    To obtain these points $\widetilde{w}(\varepsilon,M)$, the key idea is to compare the growth of $f$, $r$, and $g$.
    This is also where the hypothesis $1 \leq m < d < \ell$ is used.
    This comparison is summarized in the following lemma, which we prove after finishing the proof of \Cref{lemma:basin-at-inf-2}.

    \begin{lemma}\label{lemma:sublemma-classification}
        There exists $R > 0$ with the following property. Let $z_0 \in D(0,R)^c$, $\varepsilon > 0$, and $M \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$. Then there exist 
        \begin{itemize}
            \item a radius $0 < \delta < \min\set{\varepsilon,\delta_g}$ and an integer $n \geq M$,
            \item integers $\hat{\nu}, \hat{\mu} \geq 1$,
            \item a constant $0 < \alpha < 1$,
            \item holomorphic functions $\hat{r} \colon \mathbb{C} \times D(0,\delta_g) \to \mathbb{C}$ and $\phi \colon (\mathbb{C}\setminus\set{0})\times (D(0,\delta')\setminus\set{0}) \to \mathbb{C}$ for some $\delta' > \delta$, 
            \item  a holomorphic function $b_{\hat{\mu}} \colon D(0,\delta_g) \to \mathbb{C}$ satisfying $b_{\hat{\mu}}(0) \neq 0$ and $b_{\hat{\mu}}(w) \in D\paren*{b_{\hat{\mu}}(0), \frac{\abs{b_{\hat{\mu}}(0)}}{2}}$ for all $w \in \overline{D(0,\delta)}$,
        \end{itemize}
        such that for all $w \in \partial D(0,\delta)$, writing $(z_n,w_n) = F^n(z_0,w)$, the following hold:
        \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*), itemsep=2ex, topsep=1ex]
            \item \label{prop:r-dominating}
            $\displaystyle \abs*{f(z_n) + r(z_n,w_n) - z_n^d\hat{r}(z_n,w_n)} \leq \alpha \abs{z_n^d\hat{r}(z_n,w_n)};$
            \item \label{prop:r-factorization}
            $\displaystyle \hat{r}(z_n,w_n) = w_n^{\hat{\nu}} z_n^{\hat{\mu}} b_{\hat{\mu}}(w_n) ( 1 + \phi(z_n,w_n) );$
            \item \label{prop:phi-small}
            $\displaystyle \abs{\phi(z_n,w_n)} \leq \tfrac{1}{2};$
            \item \label{prop:zn-nonzero}
            $\displaystyle z_n \neq 0.$
        \end{enumerate}
    \end{lemma}
    We now prove the existence of the $\widetilde{w} \in D(0,\varepsilon)$ asserted in \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-7}.
    Let $R > 0$ be as in \Cref{lemma:sublemma-classification}.
    Fix any $z_0 \in D(0,R)^c$, $\varepsilon > 0$, and $M \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$, and let $\delta$, $n$, $\hat{\nu}$, $\hat{\mu}$, $\alpha$, $\hat{r}$, $\phi$, and $b_{\hat{\mu}}$ be as provided by \Cref{lemma:sublemma-classification} for these choices.
    For $k \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$, consider the holomorphic map
    \begin{equation*}
        h_k \colon D(0,\delta_g) \to \mathbb{C}, \qquad h_k(w) \coloneqq \pi_z F^k(z_0, w),
    \end{equation*}
    so that $F^k(z_0,w) = (h_k(w), g^k(w))$,
    and define the closed curves $z_k$ and $w_k$ via 
    \begin{equation*}
        (z_k(t),w_k(t)) \coloneqq (h_k(\delta e^{2\pi i t}), g^k(\delta e^{2\pi i t})) = F^k(z_0, \delta e^{2\pi i t})
    \end{equation*} 
    for $t \in [0,1]$.
    Since $D(0,\delta) \subset D(0,\varepsilon)$ and $n+1 > M$, in order to prove \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-7}, it suffices to show that $h_{n+1}$ has a zero in $D(0,\delta)$.
    By the argument principle, if the curve $z_{n+1}$ does not pass through $0$, then its winding number $\operatorname{wind}(z_{n+1}, 0)$ around $0$ is equal to the number of zeros of $h_{n+1}$ in $D(0,\delta)$, counted with multiplicity.
    Hence, it is enough to show that the curve $z_{n+1}$ does not pass through $0$ and that its winding number around $0$ satisfies $\operatorname{wind}(z_{n+1}, 0) > 0$.

    Since $(z_n(t), w_n(t)) = F^n(z_0, \delta e^{2\pi i t})$ with $\delta e^{2\pi i t} \in \partial D(0,\delta)$, properties~\ref{prop:r-dominating}--\ref{prop:zn-nonzero} of \Cref{lemma:sublemma-classification} hold at $(z_n(t), w_n(t))$ for all $t \in [0,1]$.
    In particular, $z_n$ does not pass through $0$ by property~\ref{prop:zn-nonzero}.
    Since $h_n$ is holomorphic on $D(0,\delta_g) \supset \overline{D(0,\delta)}$, the argument principle shows that $\operatorname{wind}(z_n, 0)$ equals the number of zeros of $h_n$ in $D(0,\delta)$, counted with multiplicity. 
    In particular, we have that
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:nonneg-winding}
        \operatorname{wind}(z_n, 0) \geq 0.
    \end{equation}
    Moreover, since $g$ is in normal form, $w_n(t)$ equals $\lambda^n \delta e^{2\pi i t}$ if $m = 1$ and $\delta^{m^n} e^{2\pi i m^n t}$ if $m \geq 2$.
    Hence, $w_n$ takes values in $\overline{D(0,\delta)} \setminus \set{0}$ and $\operatorname{wind}(w_n, 0) = m^n \neq 0$.

    By definition of $F$, we can write $z_{n+1} = f(z_n) + r(z_n, w_n) = \gamma_1 + \gamma_2$ for the closed curves
    \begin{equation*}
        \gamma_1(t) \coloneqq z_n(t)^d \hat{r}(z_n(t),w_n(t))
    \end{equation*}
    and
    \begin{equation*}
        \gamma_2(t) \coloneqq f(z_n(t)) + r(z_n(t), w_n(t)) - z_n(t)^d \hat{r}(z_n(t),w_n(t)).
    \end{equation*}
    We will reduce the winding number of $z_{n+1}$ to that of $\gamma_1$.
    First, observe that by property~\ref{prop:r-factorization}, $\gamma_1$ factorizes as
    \begin{equation*}
        \gamma_1(t) = z_n(t)^{d+\hat{\mu}} w_n(t)^{\hat{\nu}} b_{\hat{\mu}}(w_n(t))  (1+\phi(z_n(t),w_n(t)))
    \end{equation*}
    for all $t \in [0,1]$.
    None of the four factors passes through $0$. 
    For $z_n$, this follows by property~\ref{prop:zn-nonzero}, and for $w_n$, this was shown above. 
    Next, $b_{\hat{\mu}}(w_n)$ takes values in the disk $D\paren*{b_{\hat{\mu}}(0), \abs{b_{\hat{\mu}}(0)}/2}$, which does not contain $0$ since $b_{\hat{\mu}}(0) \neq 0$. 
    Similarly, $1 + \phi(z_n,w_n)$ only takes values in $\overline{D(1,1/2)}$ by property~\ref{prop:phi-small}.
    In particular, since each of the last two factors stays in a disk away from $0$, their winding numbers around $0$ vanish.
    As winding numbers around $0$ add under multiplication, we obtain using \eqref{eq:nonneg-winding} that
    \begin{equation*}
        \operatorname{wind}(\gamma_1, 0) = (d+\hat{\mu}) \operatorname{wind}(z_n,0) + \hat{\nu} \operatorname{wind}(w_n,0) \geq \hat{\nu} m^n > 0.
    \end{equation*}
    Finally, property~\ref{prop:r-dominating} yields that $\abs{\gamma_2(t)} \leq \alpha \abs{\gamma_1(t)} < \abs{\gamma_1(t)}$ for all $t \in [0,1]$, where we used that $0 < \alpha < 1$ and that $\gamma_1$ does not pass through $0$ as established above.
    Hence, the map $(t,u) \mapsto \gamma_1(t) + u\gamma_2(t)$, where $t,u\in[0,1]$, is a homotopy in $\mathbb{C}\setminus\set{0}$ between $\gamma_1$ and $z_{n+1} = \gamma_1 + \gamma_2$.
    In particular, $z_{n+1}$ does not pass through $0$, and since the winding number is invariant under homotopy,
    \begin{equation*}
        \operatorname{wind}(z_{n+1}, 0) = \operatorname{wind}(\gamma_1, 0) > 0.
    \end{equation*}
    By the argument principle, $h_{n+1}$ has a zero $\widetilde w \in D(0,\delta)$, that is, $\pi_z F^{n+1}(z_0, \widetilde w) = 0$, which finishes the proof.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lemma:sublemma-classification}
        There exists $R > 0$ with the following property. Let $z_0 \in D(0,R)^c$, $\varepsilon > 0$, and $M \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$. Then there exist 
        \begin{itemize}
            \item a radius $0 < \delta < \min\set{\varepsilon,\delta_g}$ and an integer $n \geq M$,
            \item integers $\hat{\nu}, \hat{\mu} \geq 1$,
            \item a constant $0 < \alpha < 1$,
            \item holomorphic functions $\hat{r} \colon \mathbb{C} \times D(0,\delta_g) \to \mathbb{C}$ and $\phi \colon (\mathbb{C}\setminus\set{0})\times (D(0,\delta')\setminus\set{0}) \to \mathbb{C}$ for some $\delta' > \delta$, 
            \item  a holomorphic function $b_{\hat{\mu}} \colon D(0,\delta_g) \to \mathbb{C}$ satisfying $b_{\hat{\mu}}(0) \neq 0$ and $b_{\hat{\mu}}(w) \in D\paren*{b_{\hat{\mu}}(0), \frac{\abs{b_{\hat{\mu}}(0)}}{2}}$ for all $w \in \overline{D(0,\delta)}$,
        \end{itemize}
        such that for all $w \in \partial D(0,\delta)$, writing $(z_n,w_n) = F^n(z_0,w)$, the following hold:
        \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*), itemsep=2ex, topsep=1ex]
            \item \label{prop:r-dominating}
            $\displaystyle \abs*{f(z_n) + r(z_n,w_n) - z_n^d\hat{r}(z_n,w_n)} \leq \alpha \abs{z_n^d\hat{r}(z_n,w_n)};$
            \item \label{prop:r-factorization}
            $\displaystyle \hat{r}(z_n,w_n) = w_n^{\hat{\nu}} z_n^{\hat{\mu}} b_{\hat{\mu}}(w_n) ( 1 + \phi(z_n,w_n) );$
            \item \label{prop:phi-small}
            $\displaystyle \abs{\phi(z_n,w_n)} \leq \tfrac{1}{2};$
            \item \label{prop:zn-nonzero}
            $\displaystyle z_n \neq 0.$
        \end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
The proof is elementary but computationally involved. 
    The main task is to find suitable bounds and normalizations for the terms of $f$, $g$, and $r$, which allow us to reduce the analysis to the terms that dominate the growth.

    We first consider the case where $g$ has a superattracting fixed point at $0$, that is, $m \geq 2$.
    The attracting case, $m=1$, is very similar.
    We sketch it at the end of the proof.

    Write $f(z) = \sum_{j=0}^{d} a_j z^j$ for $a_0, \dots, a_d \in \mathbb{C}$, $a_d \neq 0$. 
    Up to rescaling in $z$, we may assume that $a_d = 1$.
    The function $r$ can be written as $ r(z,w) = \sum_{j = 0}^{\ell} r_j(w) z^j$ where each $r_j$ is holomorphic with $r_j(0) = 0$ and $r_\ell \not\equiv 0$.

    Define
    \begin{equation*}
        \hat{r}(z,w) \coloneqq \frac{r(z,w)- \sum_{j = 0}^{d} r_j(w) z^j}{z^d} = \sum_{j=1}^{\ell-d} r_{d+j}(w) z^{j}.
    \end{equation*}
    Let $\nu_{d+j} \geq 1$ denote the order of the zero of the function $r_{d+j}$ at $0$.
    By convention, we set $\nu_{d+j} = \infty$ if $r_{d+j} \equiv 0$.
    Thus, if $\nu_{d+j} < \infty$, then $r_{d+j}(w) = w^{\nu_{d+j}} b_{j}(w)$ for some holomorphic function $b_{j}\colon D(0,\delta_g) \to \mathbb{C}$ with $b_{j}(0) \neq 0$.
    Set
    \begin{equation*}
        \mathcal{R} \coloneqq \set{ (\nu_{d+j}, j) \mid j \in\set{1, \dots, \ell-d} \text{ with } \nu_{d+j} \neq \infty}.
    \end{equation*}
    This set is nonempty since $r_\ell \not\equiv 0$ implies $\nu_{\ell} \neq \infty $.
    Furthermore, observe that $\mathcal{R}$ has at most $\ell-d$ elements.
    The function $\hat{r}$ can then be written as
    \begin{equation*}
        \hat{r}(z,w) = \sum_{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}} b_{\mu}(w) w^{\nu}z^{\mu}.
    \end{equation*}

    Later, we will need the term that contributes the most to the perturbation, which is the term with the largest ratio of the exponent of $z$ to that of $w$. 
    To that end, let $(\hat{\nu}, \hat{\mu}) \in \mathcal{R}$ be such that
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item $\displaystyle \frac{\hat{\mu}}{\hat{\nu}} \geq \frac{\mu}{\nu}$ for all $(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}$, and 
        \item $\displaystyle \hat{\mu} \geq \mu$ for all $(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}$ with $\displaystyle \frac{\hat{\mu}}{\hat{\nu}} = \frac{\mu}{\nu}$.
    \end{enumerate}
    Set $\hat{q} \coloneqq \frac{\hat{\mu}}{\hat{\nu}}$.
    We define $\mathcal{R}_{1} \coloneqq \set{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R} \mid \frac{\hat{\mu}}{\hat{\nu}} = \frac{\mu}{\nu} \text{ and } \mu < \hat{\mu}}$ and $\mathcal{R}_{2} \coloneqq \set{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R} \mid \frac{\hat{\mu}}{\hat{\nu}} > \frac{\mu}{\nu}} $.
    Then, $\mathcal{R}$ can be written as the disjoint union $\mathcal{R} = \set*{(\hat{\nu}, \hat{\mu})} \sqcup \mathcal{R}_1 \sqcup \mathcal{R}_2$.
    If $\mathcal{R}_1$ or $\mathcal{R}_2$ is empty, the corresponding steps in the proof can be skipped.

    By continuity of the functions $b_{\mu}$, there exists $\delta_b > 0$ such that for all $w \in D(0,\delta_b)$ and $(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:b-nearconstant}
        \abs{b_{\mu}(w) - b_{\mu}(0) } < \frac{\abs{b_{\mu}(0)}}{2}.
    \end{equation}
    
    Next, we set $B_{+} \coloneqq \frac{3}{2}  \max_{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}}  \abs{b_{\mu}(0)} > 0 $ and $B_{-} \coloneqq \frac{1}{2} \min_{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}} \abs{b_{\mu}(0)} > 0$.
    Observe that $\frac{2(\ell-d)B_{+}}{B_{-}} \geq 1$.
    Replacing $\frac{3}{2}$ by a larger constant if necessary, we may assume that $B_{+} > 1$.
    Let $0 < C < 1$ and observe that $\frac{C}{2B_{+}(\ell-d)} < 1$.
    Since $r_j(0) = 0$ for all $j \in \set{0,\dots, d}$, continuity yields the existence of $\delta_r > 0$ such that
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-8}
        \sum_{j=0}^{d} \max_{\abs{w} \leq \delta_r} \abs{r_j(w)} \leq \frac{C}{4}.
    \end{equation}

    Set $A \coloneqq \sum_{j=0}^{d-1} \abs{a_j}$ and fix any $R_1 > \max\set*{\frac{4A}{C}, 1}$. 
    Then,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-10}
        \sum_{j=0}^{d-1} \abs{a_j} R_1^{j-d} \leq A \frac{1}{R_1} \leq \frac{C}{4}.
    \end{equation}

    Set $C_f \coloneqq A + 1$ so that $\abs{f(z)} \leq C_f \abs{z}^{d}$ for all $\abs{z} \geq 1$.
    We define 
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-def-q}
        q \coloneqq \max\paren*{\set*{\frac{\mu}{\nu} \,\middle|\, (\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}_2} \cup \set{0}}.
    \end{equation}
    Observe that $q < \hat{q} = \frac{\hat{\mu}}{\hat{\nu}}$.
    Furthermore, set $\sigma \coloneqq \max\set*{q, \frac{m}{d} \hat{q}} < \hat{q}$.

    Let $0 < \alpha < 1$.
    Since $d > m$, there exist constants $0 < C_1 < 1$ and $C_2 > 1$ such that for all $n \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$,
    \begin{align}
    C_1^{d^n} &\leq \paren*{\frac{C}{2B_{+}(\ell-d)}}^{m^n}
                    (1+C)^{-\frac{d^{n-1} - 1}{d - 1} m\hat{q}},
        \label{eq:basin-at-infinity-C1-a}\\
    C_1^{d^n} &\leq \frac{B_{-}}{2(\ell-d)B_{+}}\,
                    (1+C)^{-\frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1} q},
        \label{eq:basin-at-infinity-C1-b}\\
    C_2^{d^n} &\geq \paren*{\frac{2(C_f + 1)}{\alpha B_{-}}}^{\frac{1}{\hat{\nu}}}
                    \paren*{\frac{1+C}{1-C}}^{d\frac{d^n-1}{\hat{\nu}(d-1)}}
                    (1-C)^{-\frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1} \hat{q}},
        \quad\text{and} \label{eq:basin-at-infinity-C2-a}\\
    C_2^{d^n} &\geq \frac{2(\ell-d)B_{+}}{B_{-}}\,
                    (1-C)^{-\frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1} \hat{q}}.
        \label{eq:basin-at-infinity-C2-b}
    \end{align}
    Indeed, every factor on the right-hand sides is either a constant or of the form $\kappa^{m^n}$ or $\kappa^{\frac{d^n-1}{d-1}}$ for some $\kappa > 0$. 
    Since $d > m$, each such factor lies between $\kappa_1^{d^n}$ and $\kappa_2^{d^n}$ for suitable constants $0 < \kappa_1 \leq 1 \leq \kappa_2$.

    There exists $R_2 > 0$ large enough so that $\frac{C_1}{C_2} R_2^{\hat{q} - \sigma} > 1$ and $\frac{C_1}{R_2^{\sigma}} < 1$. 
    Hence, for all $z \in D(0,R_2)^c$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-11}
        \paren*{\frac{C_1}{C_2}\abs{z}^{\hat{q} - \sigma}}^{d^n} \to \infty
    \end{equation}
    as $n \to\infty$, and, since $d > m$, for any $\varepsilon' > 0$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-12}
        \frac{\paren*{\frac{C_1}{\abs{z}^{\sigma}}}^{d^n}}{\paren*{\frac{\varepsilon'}{2}}^{m^n}} = \paren*{ \frac{C_1}{\abs{z}^{\sigma}} \paren*{\frac{2}{\varepsilon'}}^{\paren*{\frac{m}{d}}^{n}} }^{d^n} \to 0
    \end{equation}
    as $n \to \infty$.
    Set $R \coloneqq \max\set{R_1, R_2, \frac{1}{1-C}}$ and fix any $z_0 \in D(0,R)^{c}$.
    By \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-11}, there exists $N_1 \in \mathbb{N}_{0} $ such that for all $n \geq N_1$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-N1}
        \frac{C_2^{d^n}}{\abs{z_0}^{\hat{q}d^n}} < \frac{C_1^{d^n}}{\abs{z_0}^{\sigma d^n}}.
    \end{equation}
    Moreover, if we set $\varepsilon' \coloneqq \min\set{\varepsilon, \delta_b, \delta_r, \delta_g,1}$, then \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-12} yields $N_2 \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$ such that for all $n \geq N_2$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-N2}
        \frac{C_1^{d^n}}{\abs{z_0}^{\sigma d^n}} < \paren*{\frac{\varepsilon'}{2}}^{m^n}.
    \end{equation}
    Set $N \coloneqq \max\set{N_1, N_2, M}$, and fix any $n \geq N$.

    By \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-N1} and \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-N2}, there exists $0 < \delta < \frac{\varepsilon'}{2} < \min\set{\varepsilon, \delta_b, \delta_r, \delta_g, 1}$ such that
    \begin{equation*}
        \frac{C_2^{d^n}}{\abs{z_0}^{\hat{q}d^n}} < \delta^{m^n} < \frac{C_1^{d^n}}{\abs{z_0}^{\sigma d^n}}.
    \end{equation*}
    By \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-C1-a}, \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-C1-b}, \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-C2-a}, and \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-C2-b} respectively, it follows that
    \begin{align}
        \delta^{m^n} (1+C)^{\frac{d^{n-1} - 1}{d - 1} m\hat{q}} \abs{z_0}^{d^{n-1} m\hat{q}}
            &\leq \paren*{\frac{C}{2B_{+}(\ell-d)}}^{m^n},
            \label{eq:basin-at-infinity-cond1}\\
        \delta^{m^n} (1+C)^{\frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1} q} \abs{z_0}^{d^n q}
            &\leq \frac{B_{-}}{2(\ell-d)B_{+}},
            \label{eq:basin-at-infinity-cond2}\\
        \delta^{m^n} (1-C)^{\frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1} \hat{q}} \abs{z_0}^{d^n \hat{q}}
            &\geq \paren*{\frac{2(C_f + 1)}{\alpha B_{-}}}^{\frac{1}{\hat{\nu}}}
                \paren*{\frac{1+C}{1-C}}^{d\frac{d^n-1}{\hat{\nu}(d-1)}},
            \quad\text{and} \label{eq:basin-at-infinity-cond3}\\
        \delta^{m^n} (1-C)^{\frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1} \hat{q}} \abs{z_0}^{d^n \hat{q}}
            &\geq \frac{2(\ell-d)B_{+}}{B_{-}},
            \label{eq:basin-at-infinity-cond4}
    \end{align}
    where we used that $\sigma = \max\set*{q, \frac{m}{d} \hat{q}}$ and $\abs{z_0} \geq 1$.

    In the following, for $w \in D(0,\delta_g)$, we denote the $k$th iterate of $(z_0, w)$ by $(z_k,w_k) = F^k(z_0,w)$.
    The preparations above yield a growth estimate for the first $n$ iterates as described in the following claim.
    \begin{claim}\label{claim:basin-at-infinity-1}
        For all $k \in \set{0, \dots, n}$ and all $w \in \overline{D(0,\delta)}$, it holds that
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-13}
            (1 - C)^{\frac{d^k - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{d^k} \leq \abs{z_k} \leq (1 + C)^{\frac{d^k - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{d^k}.
        \end{equation}
    \end{claim}
    \begin{subproof}
        We proceed by induction.
        Fix any $w \in \overline{D(0,\delta)}$.
        The base case, $k = 0$, is evident.
        Assume that the statement is true for some $0 \leq k \leq n - 1$.
        In particular, it holds that $\abs{z_k} \geq \abs{z_0} \geq R$ since we assumed that $R (1-C) \geq 1$.
        Furthermore,
        \begin{equation*}
            \abs{\hat{r}(z_k, w_k)} = \abs*{\sum_{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}} b_{\mu}(w_k) w_k^{\nu} z_k^{\mu} }  
             
            \leq \sum_{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}} \abs{b_{\mu}(w_k)} \abs{w_k}^{\nu} \abs{z_k}^{\mu} 
             
            \leq B_{+} \sum_{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}} \abs{w_k}^{\nu} \abs{z_k}^{\mu}.
        \end{equation*}
        Then, for any $(\nu,\mu)\in\mathcal{R}$,
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{w_k}^{\nu} \abs{z_k}^{\mu} &\leq \abs{w_n}^{\frac{\nu}{m^{n-k}}} (1+C)^{\frac{d^k - 1}{d - 1}\mu} \abs{z_0}^{d^k\mu} \\
             
            &= \paren*{\abs{w_n} (1+C)^{\frac{d^k - 1}{d - 1}m^{n-k}\frac{\mu}{\nu}} \abs{z_0}^{d^km^{n-k}\frac{\mu}{\nu}}}^{\frac{\nu}{m^{n-k}}}
        \end{align*}
        and using \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-cond1},
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{w_n} (1+C)^{\frac{d^k - 1}{d - 1}m^{n-k}\frac{\mu}{\nu}} \abs{z_0}^{d^km^{n-k}\frac{\mu}{\nu}} &\leq \delta^{m^n} (1+C)^{\frac{d^{n-1} - 1}{d - 1}m\frac{\mu}{\nu}} \abs{z_0}^{d^{n-1} m \frac{\mu}{\nu}} \\
             
            &\leq \delta^{m^n} (1+C)^{\frac{d^{n-1} - 1}{d - 1}m \hat{q}} \abs{z_0}^{d^{n-1} m \hat{q}} \\
             
            &\leq \paren*{\frac{C}{2B_{+}(\ell-d)}}^{m^n}.
        \end{align*}
        Thus, it follows from $\frac{C}{2B_{+}(\ell-d)} < 1$ that
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{w_k}^{\nu} \abs{z_k}^{\mu} &\leq \paren*{\frac{C}{2B_{+}(\ell-d)}}^{m^k \nu} \leq \frac{C}{2B_{+}(\ell-d)}.
        \end{align*}
        Together, the above inequalities yield
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{\hat{r}(z_k, w_k)} &\leq B_{+} \sum_{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}} \abs{w_k}^{\nu} \abs{z_k}^{\mu} \leq B_{+} \sum_{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}} \frac{C}{2B_{+}(\ell-d)} \leq \frac{C}{2}.
        \end{align*}
        Consequently, combining this inequality with \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-8} and \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-10}, we obtain that
        \begin{align*}
            \abs*{z_{k+1}} &= \abs{z_k}^d \abs*{1 + \sum_{j=0}^{d-1} a_j z_k^{j-d} + \sum_{j=0}^{d} r_j(w_k) z_k^{j-d} + \hat{r}(z_k,w_k)} \\
             
            &\leq \abs{z_k}^d \paren*{1 + \sum_{j=0}^{d-1}\abs{a_j} \abs{z_k}^{j-d} + \sum_{j=0}^{d} \abs{r_j(w_k)} \abs{z_k}^{j-d} + \abs*{\hat{r}(z_k,w_k)}} \\
             
            &\leq \abs{z_k}^d \paren*{1 + \sum_{j=0}^{d-1}\abs{a_j} R^{j-d} + \sum_{j=0}^{d} \abs{r_j(w_k)} R^{j-d} + \frac{C}{2}} \\
             
            &\leq \abs{z_k}^d \paren*{1 + \frac{C}{4} + \frac{C}{4} + \frac{C}{2}} = (1+C)\abs{z_k}^d
        \end{align*}
        and analogously,
        \begin{align*}
            \abs*{z_{k+1}} &\geq \abs{z_k}^d \paren*{1 - \sum_{j=0}^{d-1}\abs{a_j} \abs{z_k}^{j-d} - \sum_{j=0}^{d} \abs{r_j(w_k)} \abs{z_k}^{j-d} - \abs*{\hat{r}(z_k,w_k)}} \\
             
            &\geq \abs{z_k}^d \paren*{1 - \frac{C}{4} - \frac{C}{4} - \frac{C}{2}} = (1-C)\abs{z_k}^d.
        \end{align*}
        Together with the induction hypothesis, we obtain
        \begin{align*}
            \abs*{z_{k+1}} &\leq (1+C)\abs{z_k}^d \leq (1+C) \paren*{(1 + C)^{\frac{d^k - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{d^k}}^d = (1 + C)^{\frac{d^{k+1} - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{d^{k+1}}
        \end{align*}
        and similarly,
        \begin{equation*}
            \abs{z_{k+1}} \geq (1 - C)^{\frac{d^{k+1} - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{d^{k+1}},
        \end{equation*}
        which completes the induction.
    \end{subproof}
    \textit{Proof of Lemma~{\ref{lemma:sublemma-classification}} (continued).}
    \Cref{claim:basin-at-infinity-1} establishes the behavior for the first $n$ iterates.
    In particular, it yields property~\ref{prop:zn-nonzero} of \Cref{lemma:sublemma-classification}.
    
    We now turn to the $(n+1)$th iterate, whose behavior is controlled by one dominating term, given by the exponents $(\hat{\nu}, \hat{\mu})$.
    \begin{claim}\label{claim:basin-at-infinity-2}
        For any $w \in \partial D(0,\delta)$, it holds that
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-14}
            \abs*{f(z_n) + r(z_n,w_n) - z_n^d \hat{r}(z_n,w_n)} = \abs*{f(z_n) + \sum_{j = 0}^{d} r_j(w_n) z_n^j} \leq \alpha \abs{z_n^d \hat{r}(z_n,w_n)}
        \end{equation}
        and that
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-15}
            \hat{r}(z_n,w_n) = w_n^{\hat{\nu}} z_n^{\hat{\mu}} b_{\hat{\mu}}(w_n) ( 1 + \phi(z_n,w_n) )
        \end{equation}
        for a holomorphic function $\phi$ defined on $(\mathbb{C}\setminus\set{0})\times (D(0,\delta_b)\setminus\set{0})$ satisfying $\abs{\phi(z_n,w_n)} \leq \frac{1}{2}$ for all $w \in \partial D(0,\delta)$.
    \end{claim}

    \begin{subproof}
        Fix any $w \in \partial D(0,\delta)$. 
        Using \Cref{claim:basin-at-infinity-1}, we estimate
        \begin{align*}
            \abs*{f(z_n) + \sum_{j = 0}^{d} r_j(w_n) z_n^j} &\leq C_f \abs{z_n}^d + \sum_{j = 0}^{d} \abs{r_j(w_n)} \abs{z_n}^j \\
             
            &\leq C_f \abs{z_n}^d + \abs{z_n}^d \\
             
            &\leq (C_f + 1) (1+C)^{d \frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{d^{n+1}}
        \end{align*}
        and 
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{z_n^d \hat{r}(z_n,w_n)} \geq \abs{\hat{r}(z_n,w_n)} (1-C)^{d \frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{d^{n+1}}.
        \end{align*}
        Hence, to prove \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-14}, it suffices to show that
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-16}
            \abs{\hat{r}(z_n,w_n)} \geq \frac{C_f + 1}{\alpha} \paren*{ \frac{1+C}{1-C} }^{d \frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1}}.
        \end{equation}
        Furthermore, observe that
        \begin{align*}
            \hat{r}(z_n,w_n) &= \sum_{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}} b_{\mu}(w_n) w_n^{\nu} z_n^{\mu} \\
             
            &= w_n^{\hat{\nu}} z_n^{\hat{\mu}} b_{\hat{\mu}}(w_n) \paren*{1 + \frac{1}{b_{\hat{\mu}}(w_n)}\sum_{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}^{\star}} b_{\mu}(w_n) w_n^{\nu - \hat{\nu}} z_n^{\mu - \hat{\mu}}}
        \end{align*}
        where $\mathcal{R}^{\star} \coloneqq \mathcal{R} \setminus \set{(\hat{\nu}, \hat{\mu})}$, and define
        \begin{equation*}
            \phi(\zeta, \xi) \coloneqq \frac{1}{b_{\hat{\mu}}(\xi)}\sum_{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}^{\star}} b_{\mu}(\xi) \xi^{\nu - \hat{\nu}} \zeta^{\mu - \hat{\mu}}
        \end{equation*}
        for $\zeta \in \mathbb{C}\setminus\set{0}$ and $\xi \in D(0,\delta_b)\setminus\set{0}$.

        To complete the proof of the claim, it suffices to show that for all $(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}^{\star}$,
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-17}
            \abs{w_n}^{\nu - \hat{\nu}} \abs{z_n}^{\mu - \hat{\mu}} \leq \frac{B_{-}}{2(\ell-d)B_{+}}
        \end{equation}
        and
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-18}
            \abs{w_n}^{\hat{\nu}} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{\mu}} \geq \frac{2(C_f + 1)}{\alpha B_{-}} \paren*{ \frac{1+C}{1-C} }^{d \frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1}}.
        \end{equation}
        Indeed, inequality \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-17} then yields
        \begin{equation*}
            \abs{\phi(z_n,w_n)} \leq \frac{B_{+}}{B_{-}}\sum_{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}^{\star}} \abs{w_n}^{\nu - \hat{\nu}} \abs{z_n}^{\mu - \hat{\mu}} \leq \frac{1}{2},
        \end{equation*}
        which is the claim about the factorization in \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-15}, and together with \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-18}, it also follows that
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{\hat{r}(z_n,w_n)} &\geq \abs{w_n}^{\hat{\nu}} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{\mu}} B_{-} \paren*{1 - \abs{\phi(z_n, w_n)}} \\
             
            &\geq \frac{B_{-}}{2} \abs{w_n}^{\hat{\nu}} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{\mu}} \\
             
            &\geq \frac{C_f + 1}{\alpha} \paren*{ \frac{1+C}{1-C} }^{d \frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1}},
        \end{align*}
        which is \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-16}, thus proving \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-14}.

        To prove that \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-17} holds, we consider two cases based on the partition of $\mathcal{R}$.
        First, consider $(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}_{1}$, that is, $\frac{\mu}{\nu} = \frac{\hat{\mu}}{\hat{\nu}}$ and $\hat{\mu} > \mu$.
        In particular, $\hat{\nu} > \nu$ and $\hat{q} = \frac{\hat{\mu}}{\hat{\nu}} = \frac{\hat{\mu} - \mu}{\hat{\nu} - \nu}$.
        Then,
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{w_n}^{\nu - \hat{\nu}} \abs{z_n}^{\mu - \hat{\mu}} &= \frac{1}{\abs{w_n}^{\hat{\nu} - \nu} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{\mu} - \mu}} = \paren*{\frac{1}{\abs{w_n} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{q}}}}^{\hat{\nu} - \nu}.
        \end{align*}
        Using \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-cond4}, we obtain
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{w_n} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{q}} &\geq \delta^{m^n} (1-C)^{\frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1} \hat{q}} \abs{z_0}^{d^n \hat{q}} \geq \frac{2 (\ell-d)B_{+}}{B_{-}},
        \end{align*}
        and thus,
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{w_n}^{\nu - \hat{\nu}} \abs{z_n}^{\mu - \hat{\mu}} &\leq \paren*{\frac{B_{-}}{2(\ell-d)B_{+}}}^{\hat{\nu} - \nu} \leq \frac{B_{-}}{2(\ell-d)B_{+}}.
        \end{align*}
        Next, let $(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}_{2}$, that is, $\frac{\mu}{\nu} < \frac{\hat{\mu}}{\hat{\nu}}$.
        Then,
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{w_n}^{\nu - \hat{\nu}} \abs{z_n}^{\mu - \hat{\mu}} = \frac{\paren*{\abs{w_n} \abs{z_n}^{\frac{\mu}{\nu}}}^{\nu}}{\paren*{\abs{w_n} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{q}}}^{\hat{\nu}}}
             
            \leq \frac{\paren*{\abs{w_n} \abs{z_n}^{q}}^{\nu}}{\paren*{\abs{w_n} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{q}}}^{\hat{\nu}}}
             
            \leq \frac{\paren*{\delta^{m^n} (1+C)^{q \frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{qd^n} }^{\nu}}{\paren*{\delta^{m^n}(1-C)^{\hat{q} \frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{\hat{q} d^n} }^{\hat{\nu}}}.
        \end{align*}
        Since \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-cond2} yields
        \begin{equation*}
            \paren*{\delta^{m^n} (1+C)^{q \frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{qd^n} }^{\nu} \leq \paren*{\frac{B_{-}}{2(\ell-d)B_{+}}}^{\nu} \leq \frac{B_{-}}{2(\ell-d)B_{+}},
        \end{equation*}
        and \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-cond4} yields
        \begin{align*}
            \paren*{\delta^{m^n}(1-C)^{\hat{q} \frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{\hat{q} d^n} }^{\hat{\nu}} \geq \paren*{\frac{2(\ell-d)B_{+}}{B_{-}}}^{\hat{\nu}}\geq 1,
        \end{align*}
        it follows that
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{w_n}^{\nu - \hat{\nu}} \abs{z_n}^{\mu - \hat{\mu}} \leq \frac{B_{-}}{2(\ell-d)B_{+}}.
        \end{align*}
        Next, we show that \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-18} holds.
        Note that by \Cref{claim:basin-at-infinity-1} and by \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-cond3},
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{w_n} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{q}} &\geq \delta^{m^n} (1-C)^{\frac{d^{n} - 1}{d - 1} \hat{q}}\abs{z_0}^{d^n \hat{q}}
             
            \geq \paren*{\frac{2(C_f + 1)}{\alpha B_{-}}  }^{\frac{1}{\hat{\nu}}} \paren*{\frac{1+C}{1-C}}^{d\frac{d^n-1}{\hat{\nu}(d-1)}}
        \end{align*}
        which implies that
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{w_n}^{\hat{\nu}} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{\mu}} &= \paren*{ \abs{w_n} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{q}}}^{\hat{\nu}} \geq \frac{2(C_f + 1)}{\alpha B_{-}}  \paren*{\frac{1+C}{1-C}}^{d\frac{d^n-1}{d-1}},
        \end{align*}
        which is \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-18}.
        This concludes the proof of \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-14} and \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-15}.
    \end{subproof}
    \textit{Proof of Lemma~{\ref{lemma:sublemma-classification}} (continued).}
    Since
    \begin{equation*}
        r(z_n,w_n) - z_n^d \hat{r}(z_n,w_n) = \sum_{j = 0}^{d} r_j(w_n) z_n^j,
    \end{equation*}
    this finishes the proof of \Cref{lemma:sublemma-classification} in the superattracting case.

    The proof for $m = 1$ follows the same structure without any conceptual departures. 
    We give a brief overview.
    In this case, $g(w) = \lambda w$ for some $\lambda \in \mathbb{D}\setminus\set{0}$. 
    Thus, $w_n = \lambda^n w$, and, in particular, $w_k = \lambda^{k-n} w_n = \lambda^{k} w$ for any $0 \leq k \leq n$.
    Consequently, the main adjustment is that we use $\abs{\lambda}^n \delta$ instead of $\delta^{m^n}$.
    Note that now $\sigma = \max\set*{q, \frac{1}{d}\hat{q}}$.
    More precisely, \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-C1-a} is replaced by
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-C1-a-attr}
        C_1^{d^n} \leq \abs{\lambda}^n \frac{C}{2B_{+}(\ell-d)}\, (1+C)^{-\frac{d^{n-1} - 1}{d - 1} \hat{q}}.
    \end{equation}
    Such a constant $0 < C_1 < 1$ exists since $\abs{\lambda}^{\frac{d^n-1}{d-1}} \leq \abs{\lambda}^n \leq 1$.
    Moreover, \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-12} is replaced by
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-12-attr}
        \frac{\paren*{\frac{C_1}{\abs{z}^{\sigma}}}^{d^n}}{\abs{\lambda}^n\frac{\varepsilon'}{2}} = \paren*{ \frac{C_1}{\abs{z}^{\sigma}} \paren*{\frac{2}{\varepsilon'\abs{\lambda}^n}}^{\frac{1}{d^n}} }^{d^n} \to 0
    \end{equation}
    as $n \to \infty$, which holds for all $z \in D(0,R_2)^c$ since $\frac{C_1}{\abs{z}^{\sigma}} < 1$ and $\paren*{\abs{\lambda}^{-n}}^{\frac{1}{d^n}} \to 1$.
    Accordingly, \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-N2} becomes $\frac{C_1^{d^n}}{\abs{z_0}^{\sigma d^n}} < \abs{\lambda}^n \frac{\varepsilon'}{2}$, and we obtain $0 < \delta < \frac{\varepsilon'}{2}$ such that
    \begin{equation*}
        \frac{C_2^{d^n}}{\abs{z_0}^{\hat{q}d^n}} < \abs{\lambda}^n \delta < \frac{C_1^{d^n}}{\abs{z_0}^{\sigma d^n}}.
    \end{equation*}
    Since $\sigma d^n \geq \hat{q} d^{n-1}$ and $\abs{z_0} \geq 1$, \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-C1-a-attr} yields that \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-cond1} changes to
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-19}
        \abs{\lambda}^n \delta (1+C)^{\frac{d^{n-1} - 1}{d - 1} \hat{q}} \abs{z_0}^{d^{n-1} \hat{q}} \leq \abs{\lambda}^n\frac{C}{2B_{+}(\ell-d)}.
    \end{equation}
    The analogues of conditions~\eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-cond2}--\eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-cond4}, restated with $\abs{\lambda}^n \delta$ in place of $\delta^{m^n}$, follow from \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-C1-b}--\eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-C2-b} as before.

    For \Cref{claim:basin-at-infinity-1}, since $\abs{w_k} = \abs{\lambda}^{k} \abs{w} \leq \delta$, the key estimate $\abs{w_k}^{\nu} \abs{z_k}^{\mu} \leq \frac{C}{2B_{+}(\ell-d)}$ follows from
    \begin{equation*}
        \abs{w_k}^{\nu} \abs{z_k}^{\mu} \leq \paren*{\delta (1+C)^{\frac{d^k - 1}{d - 1}\frac{\mu}{\nu}} \abs{z_0}^{d^k\frac{\mu}{\nu}}}^{\nu}.
    \end{equation*}    
    For $k \leq n-1$ and $\frac{\mu}{\nu} \leq \hat{q}$, the adapted condition~\eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-19} yields 
    \begin{equation*}
        \paren*{\delta (1+C)^{\frac{d^k - 1}{d - 1}\frac{\mu}{\nu}} \abs{z_0}^{d^k\frac{\mu}{\nu}}}^{\nu} \leq \paren*{\frac{C}{2B_{+}(\ell-d)}}^{\nu} \leq \frac{C}{2B_{+}(\ell-d)},
    \end{equation*}
    and combining both gives the bound. 
    The rest of the inductive step is unchanged.

    The proof of \Cref{claim:basin-at-infinity-2} carries over verbatim, using $\abs{w_n} = \abs{\lambda}^n \delta$ for $w \in \partial D(0,\delta)$.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- claim-1: body_md -->
\label{claim:basin-at-infinity-1}
        For all $k \in \set{0, \dots, n}$ and all $w \in \overline{D(0,\delta)}$, it holds that
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-13}
            (1 - C)^{\frac{d^k - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{d^k} \leq \abs{z_k} \leq (1 + C)^{\frac{d^k - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{d^k}.
        \end{equation}
<!-- end claim-1 -->
<!-- subproof-1: body_md -->
e proceed by induction.
        Fix any $w \in \overline{D(0,\delta)}$.
        The base case, $k = 0$, is evident.
        Assume that the statement is true for some $0 \leq k \leq n - 1$.
        In particular, it holds that $\abs{z_k} \geq \abs{z_0} \geq R$ since we assumed that $R (1-C) \geq 1$.
        Furthermore,
        \begin{equation*}
            \abs{\hat{r}(z_k, w_k)} = \abs*{\sum_{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}} b_{\mu}(w_k) w_k^{\nu} z_k^{\mu} }  
             
            \leq \sum_{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}} \abs{b_{\mu}(w_k)} \abs{w_k}^{\nu} \abs{z_k}^{\mu} 
             
            \leq B_{+} \sum_{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}} \abs{w_k}^{\nu} \abs{z_k}^{\mu}.
        \end{equation*}
        Then, for any $(\nu,\mu)\in\mathcal{R}$,
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{w_k}^{\nu} \abs{z_k}^{\mu} &\leq \abs{w_n}^{\frac{\nu}{m^{n-k}}} (1+C)^{\frac{d^k - 1}{d - 1}\mu} \abs{z_0}^{d^k\mu} \\
             
            &= \paren*{\abs{w_n} (1+C)^{\frac{d^k - 1}{d - 1}m^{n-k}\frac{\mu}{\nu}} \abs{z_0}^{d^km^{n-k}\frac{\mu}{\nu}}}^{\frac{\nu}{m^{n-k}}}
        \end{align*}
        and using \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-cond1},
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{w_n} (1+C)^{\frac{d^k - 1}{d - 1}m^{n-k}\frac{\mu}{\nu}} \abs{z_0}^{d^km^{n-k}\frac{\mu}{\nu}} &\leq \delta^{m^n} (1+C)^{\frac{d^{n-1} - 1}{d - 1}m\frac{\mu}{\nu}} \abs{z_0}^{d^{n-1} m \frac{\mu}{\nu}} \\
             
            &\leq \delta^{m^n} (1+C)^{\frac{d^{n-1} - 1}{d - 1}m \hat{q}} \abs{z_0}^{d^{n-1} m \hat{q}} \\
             
            &\leq \paren*{\frac{C}{2B_{+}(\ell-d)}}^{m^n}.
        \end{align*}
        Thus, it follows from $\frac{C}{2B_{+}(\ell-d)} < 1$ that
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{w_k}^{\nu} \abs{z_k}^{\mu} &\leq \paren*{\frac{C}{2B_{+}(\ell-d)}}^{m^k \nu} \leq \frac{C}{2B_{+}(\ell-d)}.
        \end{align*}
        Together, the above inequalities yield
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{\hat{r}(z_k, w_k)} &\leq B_{+} \sum_{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}} \abs{w_k}^{\nu} \abs{z_k}^{\mu} \leq B_{+} \sum_{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}} \frac{C}{2B_{+}(\ell-d)} \leq \frac{C}{2}.
        \end{align*}
        Consequently, combining this inequality with \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-8} and \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-10}, we obtain that
        \begin{align*}
            \abs*{z_{k+1}} &= \abs{z_k}^d \abs*{1 + \sum_{j=0}^{d-1} a_j z_k^{j-d} + \sum_{j=0}^{d} r_j(w_k) z_k^{j-d} + \hat{r}(z_k,w_k)} \\
             
            &\leq \abs{z_k}^d \paren*{1 + \sum_{j=0}^{d-1}\abs{a_j} \abs{z_k}^{j-d} + \sum_{j=0}^{d} \abs{r_j(w_k)} \abs{z_k}^{j-d} + \abs*{\hat{r}(z_k,w_k)}} \\
             
            &\leq \abs{z_k}^d \paren*{1 + \sum_{j=0}^{d-1}\abs{a_j} R^{j-d} + \sum_{j=0}^{d} \abs{r_j(w_k)} R^{j-d} + \frac{C}{2}} \\
             
            &\leq \abs{z_k}^d \paren*{1 + \frac{C}{4} + \frac{C}{4} + \frac{C}{2}} = (1+C)\abs{z_k}^d
        \end{align*}
        and analogously,
        \begin{align*}
            \abs*{z_{k+1}} &\geq \abs{z_k}^d \paren*{1 - \sum_{j=0}^{d-1}\abs{a_j} \abs{z_k}^{j-d} - \sum_{j=0}^{d} \abs{r_j(w_k)} \abs{z_k}^{j-d} - \abs*{\hat{r}(z_k,w_k)}} \\
             
            &\geq \abs{z_k}^d \paren*{1 - \frac{C}{4} - \frac{C}{4} - \frac{C}{2}} = (1-C)\abs{z_k}^d.
        \end{align*}
        Together with the induction hypothesis, we obtain
        \begin{align*}
            \abs*{z_{k+1}} &\leq (1+C)\abs{z_k}^d \leq (1+C) \paren*{(1 + C)^{\frac{d^k - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{d^k}}^d = (1 + C)^{\frac{d^{k+1} - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{d^{k+1}}
        \end{align*}
        and similarly,
        \begin{equation*}
            \abs{z_{k+1}} \geq (1 - C)^{\frac{d^{k+1} - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{d^{k+1}},
        \end{equation*}
        which completes the induction.
<!-- end subproof-1 -->
<!-- claim-2: body_md -->
\label{claim:basin-at-infinity-2}
        For any $w \in \partial D(0,\delta)$, it holds that
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-14}
            \abs*{f(z_n) + r(z_n,w_n) - z_n^d \hat{r}(z_n,w_n)} = \abs*{f(z_n) + \sum_{j = 0}^{d} r_j(w_n) z_n^j} \leq \alpha \abs{z_n^d \hat{r}(z_n,w_n)}
        \end{equation}
        and that
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-15}
            \hat{r}(z_n,w_n) = w_n^{\hat{\nu}} z_n^{\hat{\mu}} b_{\hat{\mu}}(w_n) ( 1 + \phi(z_n,w_n) )
        \end{equation}
        for a holomorphic function $\phi$ defined on $(\mathbb{C}\setminus\set{0})\times (D(0,\delta_b)\setminus\set{0})$ satisfying $\abs{\phi(z_n,w_n)} \leq \frac{1}{2}$ for all $w \in \partial D(0,\delta)$.
<!-- end claim-2 -->
<!-- subproof-2: body_md -->
ix any $w \in \partial D(0,\delta)$. 
        Using \Cref{claim:basin-at-infinity-1}, we estimate
        \begin{align*}
            \abs*{f(z_n) + \sum_{j = 0}^{d} r_j(w_n) z_n^j} &\leq C_f \abs{z_n}^d + \sum_{j = 0}^{d} \abs{r_j(w_n)} \abs{z_n}^j \\
             
            &\leq C_f \abs{z_n}^d + \abs{z_n}^d \\
             
            &\leq (C_f + 1) (1+C)^{d \frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{d^{n+1}}
        \end{align*}
        and 
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{z_n^d \hat{r}(z_n,w_n)} \geq \abs{\hat{r}(z_n,w_n)} (1-C)^{d \frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{d^{n+1}}.
        \end{align*}
        Hence, to prove \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-14}, it suffices to show that
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-16}
            \abs{\hat{r}(z_n,w_n)} \geq \frac{C_f + 1}{\alpha} \paren*{ \frac{1+C}{1-C} }^{d \frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1}}.
        \end{equation}
        Furthermore, observe that
        \begin{align*}
            \hat{r}(z_n,w_n) &= \sum_{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}} b_{\mu}(w_n) w_n^{\nu} z_n^{\mu} \\
             
            &= w_n^{\hat{\nu}} z_n^{\hat{\mu}} b_{\hat{\mu}}(w_n) \paren*{1 + \frac{1}{b_{\hat{\mu}}(w_n)}\sum_{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}^{\star}} b_{\mu}(w_n) w_n^{\nu - \hat{\nu}} z_n^{\mu - \hat{\mu}}}
        \end{align*}
        where $\mathcal{R}^{\star} \coloneqq \mathcal{R} \setminus \set{(\hat{\nu}, \hat{\mu})}$, and define
        \begin{equation*}
            \phi(\zeta, \xi) \coloneqq \frac{1}{b_{\hat{\mu}}(\xi)}\sum_{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}^{\star}} b_{\mu}(\xi) \xi^{\nu - \hat{\nu}} \zeta^{\mu - \hat{\mu}}
        \end{equation*}
        for $\zeta \in \mathbb{C}\setminus\set{0}$ and $\xi \in D(0,\delta_b)\setminus\set{0}$.

        To complete the proof of the claim, it suffices to show that for all $(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}^{\star}$,
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-17}
            \abs{w_n}^{\nu - \hat{\nu}} \abs{z_n}^{\mu - \hat{\mu}} \leq \frac{B_{-}}{2(\ell-d)B_{+}}
        \end{equation}
        and
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-18}
            \abs{w_n}^{\hat{\nu}} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{\mu}} \geq \frac{2(C_f + 1)}{\alpha B_{-}} \paren*{ \frac{1+C}{1-C} }^{d \frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1}}.
        \end{equation}
        Indeed, inequality \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-17} then yields
        \begin{equation*}
            \abs{\phi(z_n,w_n)} \leq \frac{B_{+}}{B_{-}}\sum_{(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}^{\star}} \abs{w_n}^{\nu - \hat{\nu}} \abs{z_n}^{\mu - \hat{\mu}} \leq \frac{1}{2},
        \end{equation*}
        which is the claim about the factorization in \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-15}, and together with \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-18}, it also follows that
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{\hat{r}(z_n,w_n)} &\geq \abs{w_n}^{\hat{\nu}} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{\mu}} B_{-} \paren*{1 - \abs{\phi(z_n, w_n)}} \\
             
            &\geq \frac{B_{-}}{2} \abs{w_n}^{\hat{\nu}} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{\mu}} \\
             
            &\geq \frac{C_f + 1}{\alpha} \paren*{ \frac{1+C}{1-C} }^{d \frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1}},
        \end{align*}
        which is \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-16}, thus proving \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-14}.

        To prove that \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-17} holds, we consider two cases based on the partition of $\mathcal{R}$.
        First, consider $(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}_{1}$, that is, $\frac{\mu}{\nu} = \frac{\hat{\mu}}{\hat{\nu}}$ and $\hat{\mu} > \mu$.
        In particular, $\hat{\nu} > \nu$ and $\hat{q} = \frac{\hat{\mu}}{\hat{\nu}} = \frac{\hat{\mu} - \mu}{\hat{\nu} - \nu}$.
        Then,
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{w_n}^{\nu - \hat{\nu}} \abs{z_n}^{\mu - \hat{\mu}} &= \frac{1}{\abs{w_n}^{\hat{\nu} - \nu} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{\mu} - \mu}} = \paren*{\frac{1}{\abs{w_n} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{q}}}}^{\hat{\nu} - \nu}.
        \end{align*}
        Using \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-cond4}, we obtain
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{w_n} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{q}} &\geq \delta^{m^n} (1-C)^{\frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1} \hat{q}} \abs{z_0}^{d^n \hat{q}} \geq \frac{2 (\ell-d)B_{+}}{B_{-}},
        \end{align*}
        and thus,
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{w_n}^{\nu - \hat{\nu}} \abs{z_n}^{\mu - \hat{\mu}} &\leq \paren*{\frac{B_{-}}{2(\ell-d)B_{+}}}^{\hat{\nu} - \nu} \leq \frac{B_{-}}{2(\ell-d)B_{+}}.
        \end{align*}
        Next, let $(\nu,\mu) \in \mathcal{R}_{2}$, that is, $\frac{\mu}{\nu} < \frac{\hat{\mu}}{\hat{\nu}}$.
        Then,
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{w_n}^{\nu - \hat{\nu}} \abs{z_n}^{\mu - \hat{\mu}} = \frac{\paren*{\abs{w_n} \abs{z_n}^{\frac{\mu}{\nu}}}^{\nu}}{\paren*{\abs{w_n} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{q}}}^{\hat{\nu}}}
             
            \leq \frac{\paren*{\abs{w_n} \abs{z_n}^{q}}^{\nu}}{\paren*{\abs{w_n} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{q}}}^{\hat{\nu}}}
             
            \leq \frac{\paren*{\delta^{m^n} (1+C)^{q \frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{qd^n} }^{\nu}}{\paren*{\delta^{m^n}(1-C)^{\hat{q} \frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{\hat{q} d^n} }^{\hat{\nu}}}.
        \end{align*}
        Since \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-cond2} yields
        \begin{equation*}
            \paren*{\delta^{m^n} (1+C)^{q \frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{qd^n} }^{\nu} \leq \paren*{\frac{B_{-}}{2(\ell-d)B_{+}}}^{\nu} \leq \frac{B_{-}}{2(\ell-d)B_{+}},
        \end{equation*}
        and \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-cond4} yields
        \begin{align*}
            \paren*{\delta^{m^n}(1-C)^{\hat{q} \frac{d^n - 1}{d - 1}} \abs{z_0}^{\hat{q} d^n} }^{\hat{\nu}} \geq \paren*{\frac{2(\ell-d)B_{+}}{B_{-}}}^{\hat{\nu}}\geq 1,
        \end{align*}
        it follows that
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{w_n}^{\nu - \hat{\nu}} \abs{z_n}^{\mu - \hat{\mu}} \leq \frac{B_{-}}{2(\ell-d)B_{+}}.
        \end{align*}
        Next, we show that \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-18} holds.
        Note that by \Cref{claim:basin-at-infinity-1} and by \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-cond3},
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{w_n} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{q}} &\geq \delta^{m^n} (1-C)^{\frac{d^{n} - 1}{d - 1} \hat{q}}\abs{z_0}^{d^n \hat{q}}
             
            \geq \paren*{\frac{2(C_f + 1)}{\alpha B_{-}}  }^{\frac{1}{\hat{\nu}}} \paren*{\frac{1+C}{1-C}}^{d\frac{d^n-1}{\hat{\nu}(d-1)}}
        \end{align*}
        which implies that
        \begin{align*}
            \abs{w_n}^{\hat{\nu}} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{\mu}} &= \paren*{ \abs{w_n} \abs{z_n}^{\hat{q}}}^{\hat{\nu}} \geq \frac{2(C_f + 1)}{\alpha B_{-}}  \paren*{\frac{1+C}{1-C}}^{d\frac{d^n-1}{d-1}},
        \end{align*}
        which is \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-18}.
        This concludes the proof of \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-14} and \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-15}.
<!-- end subproof-2 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
Under the hypotheses of \Cref{thm:basin-inf-classification}, non-bulging for the basin at infinity implies nowhere bulging.
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
Fix any $\zeta$ in the basin at infinity of $f$.
    It suffices to show that $(\zeta,0)$ belongs to the Julia set of $F$.
    Suppose, to the contrary, that $(\zeta, 0)$ is in the Fatou set of $F$.
    Since both $\mathcal{F}(F)$ and the basin at infinity of $f$ are open, there exist $\delta_1 > 0$ and $\delta_2 > 0$ such that, with $D_1 \coloneqq D(\zeta,\delta_1)$ and $D_2 \coloneqq D(0,\delta_2)$, the closure $\overline{D_1}$ lies in the basin at infinity of $f$ and $\overline{D_1 \times D_2} \subset \mathcal{F}(F)$.
    Since $F^n(\zeta,0) = (f^n(\zeta),0) \to \infty $, the only possible limit behavior is that the iterates diverge uniformly to infinity in the $z$-coordinate on $D_1 \times D_2$.

    By \Cref{lemma:basin-inf-classification-oneway}, non-bulging implies $m < d < \ell$.
    Hence, by the proof of \Cref{lemma:basin-at-inf-2}, there exists $R > 0$ such that $D(0,R)^c$ lies in the basin at infinity of $f$, and such that for every $ z \in D(0,R)^c$, $\varepsilon > 0$, and $M \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$, there exist $\widetilde{w} \in D(0,\varepsilon)$ and $n \geq M$ satisfying \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-7}, that is,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:basin-at-infinity-7-restate}
        \pi_z F^{n}(z,\widetilde{w}) = 0.
    \end{equation}
    Since $\overline{D_1}$ is a compact subset of the basin at infinity of $f$, there exists $N \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$ with $f^N(D_1) \subset D(0,R)^c$.
    Define $z_0 \coloneqq f^N(\zeta)$ and
    \begin{equation*}
        G(z,w) \coloneqq (\phi(z,w), \psi(w)) \coloneqq F^N(z,w).
    \end{equation*}
    Since $G$ is a skew product,
    \begin{equation*}
        G(D_1 \times D_2) = \bigcup_{w \in D_2} \phi(D_1 \times \set{w}) \times \set{\psi(w)}.
    \end{equation*}
    The function $\phi(\cdot,0) - z_0 = f^N(\cdot) - f^N(\zeta)$ is not identically zero and vanishes at $\zeta \in D_1$.
    By the continuity of zeros (see, e.g., \cite[Lemma~3.2]{Range1986}), after shrinking $\delta_2$
    if necessary,
    \begin{equation*}
        z_0 \in \phi(D_1 \times \set{w}) 
    \end{equation*}
    for every $w \in D_2 = D(0,\delta_2)$.
    In particular,
    \begin{equation*}
        \set{z_0} \times \psi(D_2) \subset G(D_1 \times D_2).
    \end{equation*}
    Fix any $\varepsilon > 0$ such that $D(0,\varepsilon) \subset \psi(D_2)$ and any $M \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$.
    Choose $\widetilde{w} \in D(0,\varepsilon)$ and $n \geq M$ according to \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-7-restate}.
    Then there exists $(\hat{z}_0,\hat{w}) \in D_1 \times D_2$ with $F^N(\hat{z}_0, \hat{w}) = (z_0, \widetilde{w})$.
    Hence, by \eqref{eq:basin-at-infinity-7-restate}, 
    \begin{equation*}
        \pi_z F^{N+n}(\hat{z}_0, \hat{w}) = \pi_z F^n(z_0, \widetilde{w}) = 0.
    \end{equation*}
    Since $M$ was arbitrary, the iterates cannot diverge uniformly to infinity on $D_1 \times D_2$, contradicting the uniform divergence established above.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
Let $Q$ be a countable and dense subset of $U$ such that $f^n(q) \neq f^m(p)$ for all $n,m \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$ and for all $p \neq q$ in $Q$.
    This is possible since for any fixed $q \in U$, the set $\bigcup_{n,m \in\mathbb{N}_{0}} f^{-n}(f^m(q))$ is countable.
    Hence, one can build $Q$ inductively by choosing each next point outside the countable bad set determined by the previous ones and arbitrarily close to a prescribed point of a countable dense subset of $U$.
    Furthermore, $f^n(q) \neq f^m(q)$ for all $n \neq m$ and for all $q \in Q$ since orbits of points in $U$ accumulate at infinity and therefore cannot be preperiodic.
    Let $(q_m)_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ be an enumeration of $Q$.
    
    Given any entire function $h$, we consider the skew product
    \begin{equation*}
        F_h (z,w) = (f(z) + wh(z), g(w)).
    \end{equation*}
    Observe that $ g(0) = 0 $ implies that $\set{w=0}$ is an invariant fiber of $F_h$ independent of our choice of $h$.
    The following lemma provides an entire function $h$ such that arbitrarily close to any point $(q,0)  \in Q \times \set{0}$, we find points which are mapped close to the origin after arbitrarily large numbers of iterations and points which are mapped close to infinity after arbitrarily large numbers of iterations.

    \begin{lemma}\label{lemma:nowhere-bulging-1}
        There exist an entire function $h $, a subsequence of iterates $(f^{n_k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$, and two sequences $(w_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ and $(w'_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of nonzero complex numbers converging to $0$ such that the skew product $F_h$ satisfies for all $k \geq 1$ and all $m \in \set{0,\dots, k}$,
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:nowhere-bulging-1}
            \norm{F_h^{n_k+1}(q_m, w_k) } < 1,
        \end{equation}
        and
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:nowhere-bulging-2}
            \norm{F_h^{n_k+1}(q_m, w'_k) } > k.
        \end{equation}
    \end{lemma}
    \textit{Proof of Theorem~{\ref{thm:nowhere-bulging}} (continued).}
    We now prove that $U \times \set{0}$ is contained in the Julia set $\mathcal{J}(F)$ of $F \coloneqq F_h$, where $h$ is as provided by \Cref{lemma:nowhere-bulging-1}.
    
    Assume, to the contrary, that there exists $z_0 \in U$ for which $(z_0, 0)$ belongs to the Fatou set $ \mathcal{F}(F)$ of $F$. 
    Then, the iterates of $F$ form a normal family in a neighborhood of $(z_0, 0)$. 
    In particular, one can find a compact neighborhood $C$ of $(z_0,0)$ and a subsequence $(F^{n_{k(j)} + 1})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of iterates such that this subsequence either diverges uniformly to $\infty$ on $C$ or converges uniformly to a finite limit map on $C$.

    First, assume that the subsequence diverges uniformly to infinity. 
    In particular, there exists $J \in\mathbb{N}_{0}$ such that for all $j \geq J$ and $(z,w) \in C$,
    \begin{equation*}
        \norm{F^{n_{k(j)} + 1}(z,w)} \geq 1.
    \end{equation*}
    Since $Q$ is dense in $U$ and $C$ is a neighborhood of $(z_0, 0)$, there are $\kappa \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$ and $0 < \rho < 1$ such that $\set{q_\kappa} \times D(0,\rho) \subset C$.
    Let $J' \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$ be large enough that for all $j \geq J'$, we have $\abs{w_{k(j)}} < \rho$, and therefore, $(q_\kappa, w_{k(j)}) \in C$.
    Lastly, let $J''\in \mathbb{N}_{0}$ be such that $k(j) \geq \kappa$ for all $j \geq J''$.
    Applying inequality \eqref{eq:nowhere-bulging-1}, we obtain for all $j \geq \max\set{J, J', J''} $,
    \begin{equation*}
        1 \leq \norm{F^{n_{k(j)} + 1}(q_\kappa, w_{k(j)})} < 1,
    \end{equation*}
    which is a contradiction.

    Now, assume that the subsequence of iterates has a finite limit map $\Psi\colon C \to \mathbb{C}^2$, i.e., $F^{n_{k(j)} + 1}$ converges to $\Psi$ uniformly on $C$ as $j \to \infty$. Then there exists $J \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$ with
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:nowhere-bulging-3}
        \norm{F^{n_{k(j)} + 1}(z,w) - \Psi(z,w)} \leq 1
    \end{equation}
    for all $j \geq J$ and $(z,w) \in C$. As before, we find $\kappa$, $J'$, and $ J'' \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$ with $(q_\kappa, w_{k(j)}') \in C$ for all $j \geq J'$, and with $k(j) \geq \kappa$ for all $j \geq J''$.
    Let $A \coloneqq \max_{(z,w) \in C} \norm{\Psi(z,w)} < \infty$ and choose $J'''\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$ such that $k(j) > A + 1 $ for all $j \geq J'''$.
    Applying \eqref{eq:nowhere-bulging-2} and the reverse triangle inequality, we obtain
    \begin{align*}
        \lVert F^{n_{k(j)} + 1}(q_\kappa,w_{k(j)}') - \Psi(q_\kappa,w_{k(j)}') \rVert &\geq \left\lvert \lVert F^{n_{k(j)} + 1}(q_\kappa,w_{k(j)}') \rVert - \lVert \Psi(q_\kappa,w_{k(j)}') \rVert \right\rvert \\
         
        &\geq k(j) - A \\
         
        &> A + 1 - A = 1
    \end{align*}
    for all $j \geq \max\set{J,J',J'', J'''}$, in contradiction to \eqref{eq:nowhere-bulging-3}. 
    Therefore, $(z_0,0)$ does not belong to the Fatou set of $F$, which completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lemma:nowhere-bulging-1}
        There exist an entire function $h $, a subsequence of iterates $(f^{n_k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$, and two sequences $(w_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ and $(w'_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of nonzero complex numbers converging to $0$ such that the skew product $F_h$ satisfies for all $k \geq 1$ and all $m \in \set{0,\dots, k}$,
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:nowhere-bulging-1}
            \norm{F_h^{n_k+1}(q_m, w_k) } < 1,
        \end{equation}
        and
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:nowhere-bulging-2}
            \norm{F_h^{n_k+1}(q_m, w'_k) } > k.
        \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
By the hypotheses, there exist $z_0 \in U$ and a subsequence of iterates $(f^{n_k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ with $(n_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ strictly increasing such that $f^{n_k}(z_0) \to \infty$ as $k \to \infty$.
    By normality on $U$, up to considering a subsequence, it follows that
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:nowhere-bulging-4}
        \abs{f^{n_k}(q_m)} \to \infty
    \end{equation}
    as $k \to \infty$ for all $m \in\mathbb{N}_{0}$.

    The function $h$ will be defined as the limit of a sequence of entire functions which are inductively constructed using Runge's Theorem~\ref{thm:runge-1}. 
    To that end, we require several sequences of sets which are described in the following claim and shown in Figure~\ref{fig:nowhere-bulging-sets}.

    \begin{claim}\label{claim:nowhere-bulging}
        Up to passing to a subsequence of $(f^{n_k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$, there exist a sequence of nonnegative numbers $(R_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ and a sequence of positive numbers $(\rho_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ such that letting $\widetilde{H}_k \coloneqq \overline{D(0,R_k)}$ and $\Delta^j_{m,k} \coloneqq \overline{D(f^j(q_m), \rho_k)}$, the following hold for every $k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$:
        \begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
            \item $R_k \geq k$,
            \item the sets $\Delta^j_{m,k}$, $m = 0,\dots,k$, $j = 0, \dots, n_k$, are pairwise disjoint,
            \item we have the inclusion
            \begin{equation*}
                \widetilde{H}_k \cup \bigcup_{m = 0}^k \bigcup_{j = 0}^{n_k} \Delta^j_{m,k} \subset \widetilde{H}_{k+1},
            \end{equation*}
            \item $\widetilde{H}_k$ and $ \Delta^{n_k}_{0,k} \cup \dots \cup \Delta^{n_k}_{k,k}$ are disjoint,
            \item for all $z, z' \in \Delta^j_{m,k}$, $m = 0,\dots,k$, and $j = 0, \dots, n_k$,
            \begin{equation}\label{eq:nowhere-bulging-5}
                \abs{f(z) - f(z')} < \frac{1}{4}.
            \end{equation}
        \end{enumerate}
    \end{claim}

    \begin{figure}[ht]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[
            scale=1,
            orbit/.style={->, >=stealth, dashed, shorten >=6pt, shorten <=6pt},
        ]
            \def0.18{0.18}

                                
            \draw[dashed] (0,0) circle (4.2);
            \node[left] at (80:3.8) {$\widetilde{H}_{2}$};

                            
            \filldraw[fill=gray!25] (0,0) circle (2);
            \node at (80:1.55) {$\widetilde{H}_1$};
            \fill (0,0) circle (1pt) node[below right] {$0$};

                          
            \coordinate (a0) at (0.6,0.4);
            \coordinate (a1) at (1.3,-1.0);
            \coordinate (a2) at (2.6,0.9);
            \coordinate (a3) at (3.4,-1.6);

                          
            \coordinate (b0) at (-0.9,0.8);
            \coordinate (b1) at (-1.4,-0.5);
            \coordinate (b2) at (-0.6,-1.4);
            \coordinate (b3) at (-3.0,-2.2);

                                  
            \foreach \p in {a0,a1,a2,b0,b1,b2}
                \draw[draw, fill=gray!25] (\p) circle (0.18);

                                      
            \foreach \p in {a3,b3}
                \draw[draw, fill=white] (\p) circle (0.18);

                     
            \foreach \p in {a0,a1,a2,a3,b0,b1,b2,b3}
                \fill (\p) circle (0.8pt);

                    
            \draw[orbit] (a0) to[bend left=25]  (a1);
            \draw[orbit] (a1) to[bend right=25] (a2);
            \draw[orbit] (a2) to[bend left=25]  (a3);
            \draw[orbit] (b0) to[bend right=25] (b1);
            \draw[orbit] (b1) to[bend right=25] (b2);
            \draw[orbit] (b2) to[bend left=25]  (b3);

                    
            \node[above=3pt] at (a0) {$q_0$};
            \node[above=3pt] at (b0) {$q_1$};
            \node[left=5pt] at (a3) {$f^{3}(q_0)$};
            \node[above=5pt]  at (b3) {$f^{3}(q_1)$};
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{The sets from Claim~\ref{claim:nowhere-bulging} for $k = 1$ and $n_k = 3$.
        The disks $\Delta^{n_k}_{m,k}$ around $f^{n_k}(q_m)$ lie outside $\widetilde{H}_k$ but inside $\widetilde{H}_{k+1}$.}
        \label{fig:nowhere-bulging-sets}
        \end{figure}

    \begin{subproof}[Proof of Claim~{\ref{claim:nowhere-bulging}}]
        The proof is constructive. 
        We redefine $n_0 \coloneqq 0$.
        Let $R_0 \coloneqq 0 $ so that $\widetilde{H}_0 = \set{0}$. 
        Up to conjugating by a translation, we may assume that $q_0 \neq 0$.
        Choose any $\rho_0 > 0$ with $ 0 \not\in \overline{D(q_0, \rho_0)} = \Delta^{0}_{0,0}$ and so small that \eqref{eq:nowhere-bulging-5} holds.

        Assuming that $R_0, \dots, R_k$, $n_0, \dots, n_k$, and $\rho_0, \dots, \rho_k$ have been constructed in accordance with the required properties, we construct the next elements.

        Since $\widetilde{H}_k \cup \bigcup_{m = 0}^k \bigcup_{j = 0}^{n_k} \Delta^j_{m,k}$ is bounded, there exists $R_{k+1} > k + 1$ with
        \begin{equation*}
            \widetilde{H}_k \cup \bigcup_{m = 0}^k \bigcup_{j = 0}^{n_k} \Delta^j_{m,k} \subset \overline{D(0, R_{k+1})} = \widetilde{H}_{k+1}.
        \end{equation*}
        By \eqref{eq:nowhere-bulging-4}, there exists $\ell \geq k+1$ such that
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:nowhere-bulging-6}
            f^{n_\ell}(q_m) \in \mathbb{C} \setminus \widetilde{H}_{k+1}
        \end{equation}
        for all $ m \in \set{ 0,\dots, k+1 }$. 
        Up to passing to a subsequence of iterates, we may assume that $\ell = k+1$.
        Since, by our assumption on $Q$, the points $f^j(q_m)$, $0 \leq j \leq n_{k+1}$ and $0 \leq m \leq k+1$, are pairwise distinct, there exists $\rho_{k+1} > 0$ such that all sets $\Delta^j_{m,k+1}$ are pairwise disjoint.
        By \eqref{eq:nowhere-bulging-6} and choosing a smaller $\rho_{k+1}$ if necessary, we may assume that $\widetilde{H}_{k+1}$ and $ \Delta^{n_{k+1}}_{0,{k+1}} \cup \dots \cup \Delta^{n_{k+1}}_{k+1,k+1}$ are disjoint, and, by uniform continuity on compact sets, that \eqref{eq:nowhere-bulging-5} holds. 
    \end{subproof}

    \textit{Proof of Lemma~{\ref{lemma:nowhere-bulging-1}} (continued).}
    For $k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$, define
    \begin{equation*}
        H_k \coloneqq \widetilde{H}_k \cup \bigcup_{m = 0}^k \bigcup_{j = 0}^{n_k - 1} \Delta^j_{m,k}.
    \end{equation*}
    Observe that each $H_k$ is compact with connected complement and that it is disjoint from the sets $ \Delta^{n_k}_{0,k}, \dots, \Delta^{n_k}_{k,k}$ by the previous claim. 
    Furthermore, $H_k \subset H_{k+1}$ and $\mathbb{C} = \bigcup_{k \in \mathbb{N}_{0}} H_k$.

    The following lemma provides the key step of the construction.
    The conditions are chosen to induce the desired behavior for selected orbits (properties~\ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-2} and \ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-3}), to allow the application of Runge's Theorem in the construction (properties~\ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-4} and \ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-5}), and to ensure that this behavior persists for the limit function (properties~\ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-6} and \ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-7}).

    \begin{lemma}\label{lemma:nowhere-bulging-2}
        There exist a sequence of entire functions $(h_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$, two sequences of nonzero complex numbers $(w_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ and $(w'_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$, and two sequences of positive real numbers $(r_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ and $(\delta_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ such that if we define $\zeta_{k,m} \coloneqq \pi_z F_{h_{k-1}}^{n_k}(q_m, w_k)$ and $\zeta'_{k,m} \coloneqq \pi_z F_{h_{k-1}}^{n_k}(q_m, w'_k)$ together with $A^k_m \coloneqq \overline{D(\zeta_{k,m}, r_k)}$ and $B^k_m \coloneqq \overline{D(\zeta'_{k,m}, r_k)}$ for $m = 0, \dots, k$, then we have for all $k \geq 1$,
        \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
            \item $\abs{w_k} \leq \frac{1}{k}$, $\abs{w'_k} \leq \frac{1}{k}$, $\delta_k < \frac{1}{4}$\label{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-1},
            \item $0 < \abs{g^{j}(w_k)} < \frac{1}{4}$ and $ 0 < \abs{g^{j}(w'_k)} < \frac{1}{4}$ for all $j \in \set{0, \dots, n_k + 1}$,
            \item for all $m \in \set{0, \dots, k}$,
            \begin{equation*}
                \norm*{h_k + \frac{1}{g^{n_k}(w_k)} f(\zeta_{k,m})}_{A_m^k} < \frac{1}{4},
