            \end{equation*} \label{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-2}
            \item for all $m \in \set{0, \dots, k}$,
            \begin{equation*}
                \norm*{h_k + \frac{1}{g^{n_k}(w_k')} (f(\zeta'_{k,m}) - (k+1))}_{B_m^k} < \frac{1}{4},
            \end{equation*} \label{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-3}
            \item $A^k_m \subset \Delta_{m,k}^{n_k}$ and $B^k_m \subset \Delta_{m,k}^{n_k}$ for all $m \in \set{0,\dots,k}$, \label{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-4}
            \item the sets $A^k_m$ and $B^k_m$ are disjoint, \label{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-5}
            \item $\norm{h_k - h_{k-1}}_{H_k} < \frac{1}{2^{k+1}} \min\set{\delta_0, \dots, \delta_k}$, \label{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-6}
            \item for any entire function $\widetilde{h}$, the inequality $\norm{h_{k-1} - \widetilde{h}}_{H_k} < \delta_k$ implies
            \begin{equation*}
                \pi_z F_{\widetilde{h}}^{n_k}(q_m, w_k) \in A_m^k
            \end{equation*} 
            and 
            \begin{equation*}
                \pi_z F_{\widetilde{h}}^{n_k}(q_m, w'_k) \in B_m^k
            \end{equation*} 
            for all $m \in \set{0, \dots, k}$. \label{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-7}
        \end{enumerate}
    \end{lemma}

    \begin{proof}[Proof of Lemma~{\ref{lemma:nowhere-bulging-2}}]
        The proof is constructive.
        To start the construction, let $w_0$ and $w'_0$ be any nonzero complex numbers in $D\paren*{0,\frac{1}{4}}$, let $h_0 \coloneqq \operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{C}}$, and choose any $ 0 < \delta_0  < \frac{1}{4}$ as well as $r_0 > 0$.

        Now, for $k \geq 0$, given any entire functions $h_0, \dots, h_k$, any nonzero complex numbers $w_0, \dots, w_k$ and $w'_0, \dots, w'_k$, and any positive real numbers $r_0, \dots, r_k$ and $\delta_0, \dots, \delta_k$ satisfying properties~\ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-1}--\ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-7}, we construct $h_{k+1}$, $w_{k+1}$, $w'_{k+1}$, $\delta_{k+1}$, and $r_{k+1}$ satisfying the same properties.

        Since $\pi_z F^j_{\widetilde{h}}(z,0) = f^j(z)$ for all $z \in \mathbb{C}$, $j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$, and $\widetilde{h}$ entire, by continuity, we can find $w_{k+1}, w'_{k+1} \in D(0,\frac{1}{k+1})\setminus \set{0}$ small enough that 
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:nowhere-bulging-7}
            \pi_z F^{j}_{h_k}(q_m,w_{k+1}) \in \mathring{\Delta}^{j}_{m,k+1} \text{ and } \pi_z F^{j}_{h_k}(q_m,w'_{k+1}) \in \mathring{\Delta}^{j}_{m,k+1}
        \end{equation}
        for all $m \in \set{0, \dots, k+1}$ and $j \in \set{0, \dots, n_{k+1}}$, and such that
        \begin{equation*}
            \abs{g^{j}(w_{k+1})} < \frac{1}{4} \text{ and } \abs{g^{j}(w'_{k+1})} < \frac{1}{4} 
        \end{equation*} 
        for all $j \in \set{0, \dots, n_{k + 1} + 1}$.
        In particular, from \eqref{eq:nowhere-bulging-7}, we deduce that
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:nowhere-bulging-8}
            \pi_z F^{j}_{h_k}(q_m,w_{k+1}) \in \mathring{H}_{k+1} \text{ and } \pi_z F^{j}_{h_k}(q_m,w'_{k+1}) \in \mathring{H}_{k+1}
        \end{equation}
        for all $m \in \set{0, \dots, k+1}$ and $j \in \set{0, \dots, n_{k+1} - 1}$. 
        Since $g$ is nonconstant, the zero at $0$ of each $g^j$, $j \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$, is isolated. 
        Hence, up to choosing smaller $w_{k+1}$ and $w'_{k+1}$, we obtain $g^{j}(w_{k+1}) \neq 0$ and $ g^{j}(w'_{k+1}) \neq 0$ for all $j \in \set{0, \dots, n_{k+1} + 1}$.
        Up to slightly perturbing $h_k$, which preserves all conditions obtained so far since these are open, we may assume that the maps
        \begin{equation*}
            w \mapsto \pi_z F^{n_{k+1}}_{h_k}(q_m,w)
        \end{equation*}
        are nonconstant for all $m \in \set{0, \dots, k+1}$.
        Up to slightly changing $w'_{k+1}$, we can then assume that
        \begin{equation*}
            \pi_z F^{n_{k+1}}_{h_k}(q_m,w_{k+1}) \neq \pi_z F^{n_{k+1}}_{h_k}(q_m,w'_{k+1})  
        \end{equation*}
        for all $m \in \set{0, \dots, k+1}$.
        Hence, we can find $r_{k+1} > 0$ such that properties~\ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-4} and \ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-5} hold for $k+1$.

        Then, by continuity of $h \mapsto \pi_z F_h^j(q_m,w)$ at $h_k$ with respect to uniform convergence on $H_{k+1}$ for $w \in \set{w_{k+1}, w'_{k+1}}$ and $m \in \set{0, \dots, k+1}$, which holds by \eqref{eq:nowhere-bulging-8}, there exists $0 < \delta_{k+1} < \frac{1}{4}$ such that $\norm{\widetilde{h} - h_k}_{H_{k+1}} < \delta_{k+1}$ ensures
        \begin{equation*}
            \pi_z F_{\widetilde{h}}^{n_{k+1}}(q_m, w_{k+1}) \in A_m^{k+1}
        \end{equation*} 
        and 
        \begin{equation*}
            \pi_z F_{\widetilde{h}}^{n_{k+1}}(q_m, w'_{k+1}) \in B_m^{k+1}
        \end{equation*} 
        for all $m \in \set{0, \dots, k+1}$.
        This yields property~\ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-7}.

        Lastly, we construct $h_{k+1}$. Observe that $K \coloneqq H_{k+1} \cup \bigcup_{m = 0}^{k+1} \paren*{A_m^{k+1} \cup B_m^{k+1}}$ satisfies all conditions of Runge's Theorem~\ref{thm:runge-1}. 
        Hence, we can approximate the holomorphic function $\Phi \colon K \to \mathbb{C}$,
        \begin{equation*}
            \Phi(z) \coloneqq
            \begin{cases}
                h_k(z) & \text{if } z \in H_{k+1}, \\
                -\frac{1}{g^{n_{k + 1}}(w_{k+1})} f(\zeta_{k+1, m}) & \text{if } z \in A_m^{k+1} \text{ for some } m, \\
                -\frac{1}{g^{n_{k + 1}}(w'_{k+1})} (f(\zeta'_{k+1, m}) - (k+1)) & \text{if } z \in B_m^{k+1}\text{ for some } m,
            \end{cases}
        \end{equation*}
        up to the error $\frac{1}{2^{k+2}} \min\set{\delta_0, \dots, \delta_{k+1}}$ by an entire function $h_{k+1}$. 
        Altogether, we obtain $w_{k+1}$, $w'_{k+1}$, $\delta_{k+1}$, $r_{k+1}$, and $h_{k+1}$ satisfying properties~\ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-1}--\ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-7}.
    \end{proof}

    \textit{Proof of Lemma~{\ref{lemma:nowhere-bulging-1}} (continued).}
    We use the sequences constructed in \Cref{lemma:nowhere-bulging-2} and show that the sequence $(h_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ has a uniform limit on $\mathbb{C}$. 
    First, we write $h_k$ as a telescoping sum. 
    Fix any $\ell \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$. 
    Then, for every $k > \ell$, we have
    \begin{equation*}
        h_k = h_\ell +  \sum_{j = \ell}^{k-1} \paren{h_{j + 1} - h_{j}}.
    \end{equation*}
    Property~\ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-6} yields
    \begin{equation*}
        \sum_{j = \ell}^{\infty} \norm{h_{j + 1} - h_{j}}_{H_{\ell}} \leq \sum_{j = \ell}^{\infty}  \norm{h_{j + 1} - h_{j}}_{H_{j+1}}  < \sum_{j = \ell}^{\infty} \frac{1}{2^{j+2}} < \infty.
    \end{equation*}
    Therefore, the partial sums form a uniform Cauchy sequence on $H_{\ell}$; hence, they have a uniform limit function $\lim_{k \to \infty} h_k = h_{\ell} + \sum_{j = \ell}^{\infty} (h_{j + 1} - h_{j})$ on $H_{\ell}$. 

    Since the compact sets $H_k$ are an increasing sequence exhausting $\mathbb{C}$, we can apply Weierstraß's Theorem~\ref{thm:weierstrass} and obtain an entire limit function $h = \lim_{k\to \infty}h_k$. 
    Moreover, for any $k \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$, this limit function satisfies
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:nowhere-bulging-9}
        \begin{split}
            \norm{h - h_k}_{H_{k+1}} &\leq \sum_{j = k}^{\infty} \norm{h_{j + 1} - h_{j}}_{H_{j+1}} 
            \leq \sum_{j = k+1}^{\infty} \frac{1}{2^{j+1}} \min\lbrace \delta_0, \dots, \delta_j \rbrace \\
             
            &\leq \sum_{j = k+1}^{\infty} \frac{1}{2^{j+1}} \delta_{k+1} < \delta_{k+1}.
        \end{split}
    \end{equation}

    Our construction of $h$ ensures that the map $F_h(z,w) = (f(z) + wh(z), g(w))$, together with the sequences $(w_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ and $(w_k')_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$, satisfies the assertions of \Cref{lemma:nowhere-bulging-1}.

    Indeed, by property~\ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-1} of \Cref{lemma:nowhere-bulging-2}, both $(w_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ and $(w_k')_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ are sequences of nonzero complex numbers with $\lim_{k\to\infty} w_k = \lim_{k\to\infty} w_k' = 0$. 

    Also, fixing any $k \geq 1$ and $m \in \set{0, \dots, k}$, inequality~\eqref{eq:nowhere-bulging-9} allows us to apply property~\ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-7}, which yields
    \begin{equation*}
        \pi_z F_{h}^{n_k}(q_m, w_k) \in A_m^k \text{ and }  \pi_z F_{h}^{n_k}(q_m, w'_k) \in B_m^k.
    \end{equation*}
    Thus, we obtain
    \begin{equation*}
        \begin{split}
            \abs{\pi_z F_{h}^{n_k+1}(q_m, w_k)} &= \abs{f(\pi_z F_{h}^{n_k}(q_m, w_k)) + g^{n_k}(w_k) h(\pi_z F_{h}^{n_k}(q_m, w_k))}\\
             
            &\leq \abs{f(\pi_z F_{h}^{n_k}(q_m, w_k)) - f(\zeta_{k,m})}  \\
             
            & \qquad + \abs{f(\zeta_{k,m}) +  g^{n_k}(w_k) h(\pi_z F_{h}^{n_k}(q_m, w_k))}\\
             
            &< \frac{1}{4} +  \abs{g^{n_k}(w_k)} \abs*{ h(\pi_z F_{h}^{n_k}(q_m, w_k)) + \frac{1}{g^{n_k}(w_k)} f(\zeta_{k,m})} \\
             
            &\leq \frac{1}{4} +  \norm*{h + \frac{1}{g^{n_k}(w_k)} f(\zeta_{k,m})}_{A_m^k}
        \end{split}
    \end{equation*}
    where we used \eqref{eq:nowhere-bulging-5} and $\abs{g^{n_k}(w_k)} < 1$.
    Observe that $A_m^k \subset \Delta_{m,k}^{n_k}\subset H_{k+1}$ by property~\ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-4} and \Cref{claim:nowhere-bulging}.
    Applying properties~\ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-1} and \ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-2} together with \eqref{eq:nowhere-bulging-9} then yields
    \begin{equation*}
        \begin{split}
            \norm*{h + \frac{1}{g^{n_k}(w_k)} f(\zeta_{k,m})}_{A_m^k} &\leq  \norm*{h - h_k}_{A_m^k} + \norm*{h_k + \frac{1}{g^{n_k}(w_k)} f(\zeta_{k,m})}_{A_m^k} \\
             
            &\leq \norm*{h - h_k}_{H_{k+1}} + \frac{1}{4} \\
             
            &< \delta_{k+1} + \frac{1}{4} < \frac{1}{2}.
        \end{split}
    \end{equation*}
    Therefore, we obtain $\abs{\pi_z F_{h}^{n_{k}+1}(q_m, w_k)} < \frac{3}{4}$, and consequently,
    \begin{equation*}
        \norm{F_{h}^{n_{k}+1}(q_m, w_k)} \leq \abs{\pi_z F_{h}^{n_{k}+1}(q_m, w_k)} + \abs{g^{n_k + 1}(w_k)} < \frac{3}{4} + \frac{1}{4} = 1,
    \end{equation*}
    which proves \eqref{eq:nowhere-bulging-1}.
    For $w'_k$, we observe that, as before,
    \begin{equation*}
        \abs{\pi_z F_{h}^{n_k+1}(q_m, w'_k) - (k+1)} \leq \frac{1}{4} + \norm*{h + \frac{1}{g^{n_k}(w'_k)} (f(\zeta'_{k,m}) - (k+1))}_{B_m^k}
    \end{equation*}
    and 
    \begin{equation*}
        \norm*{h + \frac{1}{g^{n_k}(w'_k)} (f(\zeta'_{k,m}) - (k+1))}_{B_m^k} < \frac{1}{2},
    \end{equation*}
    that is, $\pi_z F_{h}^{n_k+1}(q_m, w'_k) \in D\paren*{k+1, \frac{3}{4}}$.
    Thus,
    \begin{equation*}
        \norm{F_{h}^{n_k+1}(q_m, w'_k)} \geq \abs{\pi_z F_{h}^{n_k+1}(q_m, w'_k)} > k,
    \end{equation*}
    which completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- claim-3: body_md -->
\label{claim:nowhere-bulging}
        Up to passing to a subsequence of $(f^{n_k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$, there exist a sequence of nonnegative numbers $(R_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ and a sequence of positive numbers $(\rho_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ such that letting $\widetilde{H}_k \coloneqq \overline{D(0,R_k)}$ and $\Delta^j_{m,k} \coloneqq \overline{D(f^j(q_m), \rho_k)}$, the following hold for every $k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$:
        \begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
            \item $R_k \geq k$,
            \item the sets $\Delta^j_{m,k}$, $m = 0,\dots,k$, $j = 0, \dots, n_k$, are pairwise disjoint,
            \item we have the inclusion
            \begin{equation*}
                \widetilde{H}_k \cup \bigcup_{m = 0}^k \bigcup_{j = 0}^{n_k} \Delta^j_{m,k} \subset \widetilde{H}_{k+1},
            \end{equation*}
            \item $\widetilde{H}_k$ and $ \Delta^{n_k}_{0,k} \cup \dots \cup \Delta^{n_k}_{k,k}$ are disjoint,
            \item for all $z, z' \in \Delta^j_{m,k}$, $m = 0,\dots,k$, and $j = 0, \dots, n_k$,
            \begin{equation}\label{eq:nowhere-bulging-5}
                \abs{f(z) - f(z')} < \frac{1}{4}.
            \end{equation}
        \end{enumerate}
<!-- end claim-3 -->
<!-- subproof-3: body_md -->
he proof is constructive. 
        We redefine $n_0 \coloneqq 0$.
        Let $R_0 \coloneqq 0 $ so that $\widetilde{H}_0 = \set{0}$. 
        Up to conjugating by a translation, we may assume that $q_0 \neq 0$.
        Choose any $\rho_0 > 0$ with $ 0 \not\in \overline{D(q_0, \rho_0)} = \Delta^{0}_{0,0}$ and so small that \eqref{eq:nowhere-bulging-5} holds.

        Assuming that $R_0, \dots, R_k$, $n_0, \dots, n_k$, and $\rho_0, \dots, \rho_k$ have been constructed in accordance with the required properties, we construct the next elements.

        Since $\widetilde{H}_k \cup \bigcup_{m = 0}^k \bigcup_{j = 0}^{n_k} \Delta^j_{m,k}$ is bounded, there exists $R_{k+1} > k + 1$ with
        \begin{equation*}
            \widetilde{H}_k \cup \bigcup_{m = 0}^k \bigcup_{j = 0}^{n_k} \Delta^j_{m,k} \subset \overline{D(0, R_{k+1})} = \widetilde{H}_{k+1}.
        \end{equation*}
        By \eqref{eq:nowhere-bulging-4}, there exists $\ell \geq k+1$ such that
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:nowhere-bulging-6}
            f^{n_\ell}(q_m) \in \mathbb{C} \setminus \widetilde{H}_{k+1}
        \end{equation}
        for all $ m \in \set{ 0,\dots, k+1 }$. 
        Up to passing to a subsequence of iterates, we may assume that $\ell = k+1$.
        Since, by our assumption on $Q$, the points $f^j(q_m)$, $0 \leq j \leq n_{k+1}$ and $0 \leq m \leq k+1$, are pairwise distinct, there exists $\rho_{k+1} > 0$ such that all sets $\Delta^j_{m,k+1}$ are pairwise disjoint.
        By \eqref{eq:nowhere-bulging-6} and choosing a smaller $\rho_{k+1}$ if necessary, we may assume that $\widetilde{H}_{k+1}$ and $ \Delta^{n_{k+1}}_{0,{k+1}} \cup \dots \cup \Delta^{n_{k+1}}_{k+1,k+1}$ are disjoint, and, by uniform continuity on compact sets, that \eqref{eq:nowhere-bulging-5} holds.
<!-- end subproof-3 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lemma:nowhere-bulging-2}
        There exist a sequence of entire functions $(h_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$, two sequences of nonzero complex numbers $(w_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ and $(w'_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$, and two sequences of positive real numbers $(r_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ and $(\delta_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ such that if we define $\zeta_{k,m} \coloneqq \pi_z F_{h_{k-1}}^{n_k}(q_m, w_k)$ and $\zeta'_{k,m} \coloneqq \pi_z F_{h_{k-1}}^{n_k}(q_m, w'_k)$ together with $A^k_m \coloneqq \overline{D(\zeta_{k,m}, r_k)}$ and $B^k_m \coloneqq \overline{D(\zeta'_{k,m}, r_k)}$ for $m = 0, \dots, k$, then we have for all $k \geq 1$,
        \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
            \item $\abs{w_k} \leq \frac{1}{k}$, $\abs{w'_k} \leq \frac{1}{k}$, $\delta_k < \frac{1}{4}$\label{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-1},
            \item $0 < \abs{g^{j}(w_k)} < \frac{1}{4}$ and $ 0 < \abs{g^{j}(w'_k)} < \frac{1}{4}$ for all $j \in \set{0, \dots, n_k + 1}$,
            \item for all $m \in \set{0, \dots, k}$,
            \begin{equation*}
                \norm*{h_k + \frac{1}{g^{n_k}(w_k)} f(\zeta_{k,m})}_{A_m^k} < \frac{1}{4},
            \end{equation*} \label{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-2}
            \item for all $m \in \set{0, \dots, k}$,
            \begin{equation*}
                \norm*{h_k + \frac{1}{g^{n_k}(w_k')} (f(\zeta'_{k,m}) - (k+1))}_{B_m^k} < \frac{1}{4},
            \end{equation*} \label{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-3}
            \item $A^k_m \subset \Delta_{m,k}^{n_k}$ and $B^k_m \subset \Delta_{m,k}^{n_k}$ for all $m \in \set{0,\dots,k}$, \label{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-4}
            \item the sets $A^k_m$ and $B^k_m$ are disjoint, \label{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-5}
            \item $\norm{h_k - h_{k-1}}_{H_k} < \frac{1}{2^{k+1}} \min\set{\delta_0, \dots, \delta_k}$, \label{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-6}
            \item for any entire function $\widetilde{h}$, the inequality $\norm{h_{k-1} - \widetilde{h}}_{H_k} < \delta_k$ implies
            \begin{equation*}
                \pi_z F_{\widetilde{h}}^{n_k}(q_m, w_k) \in A_m^k
            \end{equation*} 
            and 
            \begin{equation*}
                \pi_z F_{\widetilde{h}}^{n_k}(q_m, w'_k) \in B_m^k
            \end{equation*} 
            for all $m \in \set{0, \dots, k}$. \label{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-7}
        \end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
The proof is constructive.
        To start the construction, let $w_0$ and $w'_0$ be any nonzero complex numbers in $D\paren*{0,\frac{1}{4}}$, let $h_0 \coloneqq \operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{C}}$, and choose any $ 0 < \delta_0  < \frac{1}{4}$ as well as $r_0 > 0$.

        Now, for $k \geq 0$, given any entire functions $h_0, \dots, h_k$, any nonzero complex numbers $w_0, \dots, w_k$ and $w'_0, \dots, w'_k$, and any positive real numbers $r_0, \dots, r_k$ and $\delta_0, \dots, \delta_k$ satisfying properties~\ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-1}--\ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-7}, we construct $h_{k+1}$, $w_{k+1}$, $w'_{k+1}$, $\delta_{k+1}$, and $r_{k+1}$ satisfying the same properties.

        Since $\pi_z F^j_{\widetilde{h}}(z,0) = f^j(z)$ for all $z \in \mathbb{C}$, $j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$, and $\widetilde{h}$ entire, by continuity, we can find $w_{k+1}, w'_{k+1} \in D(0,\frac{1}{k+1})\setminus \set{0}$ small enough that 
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:nowhere-bulging-7}
            \pi_z F^{j}_{h_k}(q_m,w_{k+1}) \in \mathring{\Delta}^{j}_{m,k+1} \text{ and } \pi_z F^{j}_{h_k}(q_m,w'_{k+1}) \in \mathring{\Delta}^{j}_{m,k+1}
        \end{equation}
        for all $m \in \set{0, \dots, k+1}$ and $j \in \set{0, \dots, n_{k+1}}$, and such that
        \begin{equation*}
            \abs{g^{j}(w_{k+1})} < \frac{1}{4} \text{ and } \abs{g^{j}(w'_{k+1})} < \frac{1}{4} 
        \end{equation*} 
        for all $j \in \set{0, \dots, n_{k + 1} + 1}$.
        In particular, from \eqref{eq:nowhere-bulging-7}, we deduce that
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:nowhere-bulging-8}
            \pi_z F^{j}_{h_k}(q_m,w_{k+1}) \in \mathring{H}_{k+1} \text{ and } \pi_z F^{j}_{h_k}(q_m,w'_{k+1}) \in \mathring{H}_{k+1}
        \end{equation}
        for all $m \in \set{0, \dots, k+1}$ and $j \in \set{0, \dots, n_{k+1} - 1}$. 
        Since $g$ is nonconstant, the zero at $0$ of each $g^j$, $j \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$, is isolated. 
        Hence, up to choosing smaller $w_{k+1}$ and $w'_{k+1}$, we obtain $g^{j}(w_{k+1}) \neq 0$ and $ g^{j}(w'_{k+1}) \neq 0$ for all $j \in \set{0, \dots, n_{k+1} + 1}$.
        Up to slightly perturbing $h_k$, which preserves all conditions obtained so far since these are open, we may assume that the maps
        \begin{equation*}
            w \mapsto \pi_z F^{n_{k+1}}_{h_k}(q_m,w)
        \end{equation*}
        are nonconstant for all $m \in \set{0, \dots, k+1}$.
        Up to slightly changing $w'_{k+1}$, we can then assume that
        \begin{equation*}
            \pi_z F^{n_{k+1}}_{h_k}(q_m,w_{k+1}) \neq \pi_z F^{n_{k+1}}_{h_k}(q_m,w'_{k+1})  
        \end{equation*}
        for all $m \in \set{0, \dots, k+1}$.
        Hence, we can find $r_{k+1} > 0$ such that properties~\ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-4} and \ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-5} hold for $k+1$.

        Then, by continuity of $h \mapsto \pi_z F_h^j(q_m,w)$ at $h_k$ with respect to uniform convergence on $H_{k+1}$ for $w \in \set{w_{k+1}, w'_{k+1}}$ and $m \in \set{0, \dots, k+1}$, which holds by \eqref{eq:nowhere-bulging-8}, there exists $0 < \delta_{k+1} < \frac{1}{4}$ such that $\norm{\widetilde{h} - h_k}_{H_{k+1}} < \delta_{k+1}$ ensures
        \begin{equation*}
            \pi_z F_{\widetilde{h}}^{n_{k+1}}(q_m, w_{k+1}) \in A_m^{k+1}
        \end{equation*} 
        and 
        \begin{equation*}
            \pi_z F_{\widetilde{h}}^{n_{k+1}}(q_m, w'_{k+1}) \in B_m^{k+1}
        \end{equation*} 
        for all $m \in \set{0, \dots, k+1}$.
        This yields property~\ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-7}.

        Lastly, we construct $h_{k+1}$. Observe that $K \coloneqq H_{k+1} \cup \bigcup_{m = 0}^{k+1} \paren*{A_m^{k+1} \cup B_m^{k+1}}$ satisfies all conditions of Runge's Theorem~\ref{thm:runge-1}. 
        Hence, we can approximate the holomorphic function $\Phi \colon K \to \mathbb{C}$,
        \begin{equation*}
            \Phi(z) \coloneqq
            \begin{cases}
                h_k(z) & \text{if } z \in H_{k+1}, \\
                -\frac{1}{g^{n_{k + 1}}(w_{k+1})} f(\zeta_{k+1, m}) & \text{if } z \in A_m^{k+1} \text{ for some } m, \\
                -\frac{1}{g^{n_{k + 1}}(w'_{k+1})} (f(\zeta'_{k+1, m}) - (k+1)) & \text{if } z \in B_m^{k+1}\text{ for some } m,
            \end{cases}
        \end{equation*}
        up to the error $\frac{1}{2^{k+2}} \min\set{\delta_0, \dots, \delta_{k+1}}$ by an entire function $h_{k+1}$. 
        Altogether, we obtain $w_{k+1}$, $w'_{k+1}$, $\delta_{k+1}$, $r_{k+1}$, and $h_{k+1}$ satisfying properties~\ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-1}--\ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-7}.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- theorem-7: body_md -->
\label{thm:partial-wandering}
    Let $f(z) \coloneqq z + 2\pi i + \frac{1}{2} - \frac{1}{2}\exp(z)$ and let $g$ be a nonconstant entire function with an attracting fixed point at $0$.
    Then there exists an entire function $h$ such that the skew product $F(z,w) = (f(z) + w h(z), g(w))$ has a partially bulging wandering domain.
<!-- end theorem-7 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lemma:baker}
    Let $b(z) = z + \frac{1}{2} - \frac{1}{2}\exp(z)$ and $f(z) = b(z) + 2 \pi i$.
    Then the immediate basin of attraction $U$ of $b$ at $0$ is a wandering domain for $f$.
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lemma:partial-wandering-1}
    Let $b(z) = z + \frac{1}{2} - \frac{1}{2}\exp(z)$.
    For all sufficiently large $r > 0$, the set $D_r \coloneqq D\paren*{ - \frac{1}{2r}, \frac{1}{2r} }$ is a $b$-invariant subset of $U$, that is, $b(D_r) \subset D_r$.
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
We rewrite $b$ as $b(z) = \frac{1}{2} \paren*{ z - \sum_{k = 2}^{\infty} \frac{z^k}{k!}}$.
    Using the conjugation $\zeta = \phi(z) \coloneqq \frac{1}{z}$ in a small punctured neighborhood of $0$, we obtain
    \begin{equation*}
        \begin{split}
            \zeta_1 &= \frac{2}{z \paren*{1 - \sum_{k = 2}^{\infty} \frac{z^{k-1}}{k!}}} = \frac{2}{z} \paren*{1  + \sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\paren*{\sum_{k = 2}^{\infty} \frac{z^{k-1}}{k!}}^j} \\
             
            &= 2\zeta \paren*{ 1 + \frac{1}{2\zeta} + \mathcal{O}\paren*{\frac{1}{\zeta^2}} } = 2\zeta + 1 + \mathcal{O}\paren*{\frac{1}{\zeta}}.
        \end{split}
    \end{equation*}
    Denote the conjugated function by $\widetilde{b} \coloneqq \phi \circ b \circ \phi$, and observe that left half-planes $L_r \coloneqq \set{ \zeta \in \mathbb{C} \mid \operatorname{Re} (\zeta) < -r }$ are $\widetilde{b}$-invariant for $r > 0$ sufficiently large.
    Indeed,
    \begin{equation*}
        \begin{split}
            \operatorname{Re} (\zeta_1) &= 2 \operatorname{Re} (\zeta) + 1 + \mathcal{O}\paren*{\frac{1}{\abs{\zeta}}} < -2r + 1 + \frac{r}{2} \leq -r
        \end{split}
    \end{equation*}
    for all $\zeta \in L_r$.
    Moreover, these left half-planes are mapped to disks under $\phi$. 
    More precisely, $\phi(L_r) = D_r = D\paren*{ -\frac{1}{2r}, \frac{1}{2r} }$.
    For $0 < r < R$, it holds that $D_R \subset D_r$.
    Let $r > 0$ be large enough that $L_r$ is $\widetilde{b}$-invariant and $D_r \subset U$. Then $D_r \subset U$ is invariant under $b$ since for every $z \in D_r$,
    \begin{equation*}
        b(z) = \phi \circ \widetilde{b} \circ \phi(z) \in \phi \circ \widetilde{b} \circ \phi(D_r) = \phi \circ \widetilde{b}(L_r) \subset \phi(L_r) = D_r,
    \end{equation*}
    which completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
Let $0 < \delta_g < 1$ be such that $g(D(0,\delta_g))\subset D(0,\delta_g)$ and points in $D(0,\delta_g)$ converge uniformly to $0$ under iteration of $g$. 

    By \Cref{lemma:partial-wandering-1}, there exists $r > 0$ large enough that $D_r = D\paren*{ -\frac{1}{2r}, \frac{1}{2r} } \subset U$ is a $b$-invariant disk.
    Fix $0 < \varepsilon < \frac{1}{2r}$ and define the disk $K_0 \coloneqq \overline{D\paren*{-\frac{1}{2r}, \frac{1}{2r} - \varepsilon}} \subset D_r$.
    Then $K_0 $ is compact and simply connected with nonempty interior. 
    Since $b(K_0) \subset D_r$ is compact, it has positive distance to the boundary $\partial D_r$, and hence, $\varepsilon_0 \coloneqq \frac{1}{4} \operatorname{dist}(\partial D_r, b(K_0)) > 0$. 
    Thus, 
    \begin{equation*}
        b(K_0) + \overline{D(0, \varepsilon_0)}\subset \overline{D\paren*{-\frac{1}{2r}, \frac{1}{2r} - \varepsilon_0}} =: K_1 \subset D_r.
    \end{equation*}
    Similarly, we obtain positive real numbers $\varepsilon_n$ such that the compact sets $K_{n+1} \coloneqq \overline{D\paren*{-\frac{1}{2r}, \frac{1}{2r} - \varepsilon_{n}}}$ satisfy
    \begin{equation*}
        b(K_n) + \overline{D(0, \varepsilon_{n})} \subset K_{n+1} \subset D_r.
    \end{equation*}
    By construction, all sets $K_n$ are compact, simply connected, and contained in $D_r$.
    In particular, there are $p_n > 0$ such that $\min{\abs{K_n}} > p_n$.
    Further shrinking $p_n$ if necessary, we may assume that 
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:uniform-continuity}
        \abs{f(z) - 2(n+1)\pi i} <\frac{1}{2}
    \end{equation}
    for all $z \in D(2n\pi i, p_n)$ and $n \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$.

    We now use an approach similar to \cite[Theorem 1.1]{Potthink2026} (see also the proof of \Cref{thm:nowhere-bulging}) to construct the desired function $h$.
    The idea is to force $(0,0)$ to lie in the Julia set of $F$ while keeping the perturbation small on the sets $K_n$.

    Given a sequence $(w_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of nonzero complex numbers in $D(0,\delta_g)$ with $g^k(w_k) \neq 0$ for all $k \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$, recursively define sets $\Omega_{n,0} \coloneqq \set{0}$ and $\Omega_{n,k+1} \coloneqq f(\Omega_{n,k}) - \frac{g^k(w_n)}{g^k(w_k)} 2(k+1)\pi i + g^k(w_n) D\paren*{0, \frac{1}{2}}$ for $n \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$ and $0 \leq k \leq n-1$.
    By continuity and since $f^k(0) = 2k\pi i$, there exists such a sequence $(w_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of nonzero complex numbers which converges to $0$ and satisfies $\Omega_{n,k} \subset D\paren*{2k\pi i, \frac{p_k}{2}}$ for all $n \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$ and $0 \leq k \leq n$.

    Then, Runge's Theorem~\ref{thm:runge-1} applied to the sets $K_n + 2n\pi i$ and $\overline{ D\paren*{2n\pi i, \frac{p_n}{2}}}$ as shown in Figure~\ref{fig:partial-wandering-sets}, $n \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$, yields an entire function $h$ with
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:partial-wandering-1}
        \abs{h(z)} < \varepsilon_n
    \end{equation}
    for all $z \in K_n + 2n\pi i$, $n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$, and
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:partial-wandering-2}
        \abs*{h(z) + \frac{2(n+1)\pi i}{g^n(w_n)}} < \frac{1}{2}
    \end{equation}
    for all $z \in D\paren*{2n\pi i, \frac{p_n}{2}}$, $n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$.

    \begin{figure}[ht]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[scale=1.1]
                            
            \draw[->] (0,-1.4) -- (0,7.4) node[above] {$\operatorname{Im} z$};

            \foreach \n/\varepsilon in {0/0.35, 1/0.25, 2/0.18}{
                \pgfmathsetmacro{\y}{3*\n}
                \pgfmathsetmacro{\q}{0.4*\varepsilon}
                \draw[dashed] (-1,\y) circle (1);
                               
                \filldraw[fill=gray!30] (-1,\y) circle ({1-\varepsilon});
                                   
                \filldraw[fill=gray!65] (0,\y) circle (\q);
                \fill (0,\y) circle (0.8pt);
            }

                    
            \node at (-1,0) {$K_0$};
            \node at (-1,3) {$K_1 + 2\pi i$};
            \node at (-1,6) {$K_2 + 4\pi i$};
            \node[right] at (0.2,0)  {$0$};
            \node[right] at (0.15,3) {$2\pi i$};
            \node[right] at (0.12,6) {$4\pi i$};
            \node at (-1,7.5) {$\vdots$};

                               
            \foreach \n in {0,1}{
                \pgfmathsetmacro{\y}{3*\n}
                \draw[->, >=stealth] (-2.1,\y+0.35) to[bend left=40]
                    node[left] {$f$} (-2.1,\y+2.65);
            }
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{The sets $K_n + 2n\pi i$ inside the disks $D_r + 2n\pi i$ together with the disks $D(2n\pi i, p_n/2)$ around $f^n(0) = 2n\pi i$ for $n = 0,1,2$.}
        \label{fig:partial-wandering-sets}
        \end{figure}

    We first show that $(0,0)$ lies in the Julia set of the skew product $F(z,w) = (f(z) + wh(z), g(w))$.
    Let $z \in \Omega_{n,k}$ for some $k < n$.
    Then, from $\Omega_{n,k} \subset D\paren*{2k\pi i, \frac{p_k}{2}}$ and \eqref{eq:partial-wandering-2}, it follows that
    \begin{equation*}
        \begin{split}
            \pi_z F(z, g^k(w_n)) &= f(z) + g^k(w_n)h(z) \\
             
            &\in f(\Omega_{n,k}) + g^k(w_n) \paren*{- \frac{2(k+1)\pi i}{g^k(w_k)} + D\paren*{0,\frac{1}{2}}} \\
             
            &= f(\Omega_{n,k}) - \frac{g^k(w_n)}{g^k(w_k)} 2(k+1)\pi i + g^k(w_n) D\paren*{0, \frac{1}{2}} = \Omega_{n,k+1}.
        \end{split}
    \end{equation*}
    By induction on $k$, it follows that $\pi_z F^k(0, w_n) \in \Omega_{n,k}$ for $0 \leq k \leq n$, using $F^k(0,w_n) = (\pi_z F^k(0,w_n), g^k(w_n))$ and the inclusion just computed.
    Hence, setting $\zeta_n \coloneqq \pi_z F^n(0,w_n) $ so that $ \zeta_n \in D\paren*{2n\pi i, \frac{p_n}{2}}$, this implies
    \begin{equation*}
        \begin{split}
            \abs{\pi_z F^{n+1}(0,w_n)} &= \abs{f(\zeta_n) + g^n(w_n)h(\zeta_n)} \\
             
            &\leq \abs*{f(\zeta_{n}) - 2(n+1)\pi i} + \abs{g^n(w_n)}\abs*{h(\zeta_n) + \frac{2(n+1)\pi i}{g^n(w_n)}} \\
             
            &\leq \abs*{f(\zeta_{n}) - 2(n+1)\pi i} + \frac{\abs{g^n(w_n)}}{2} \\  
             
            &< \frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{2} = 1,
        \end{split}
    \end{equation*}
    where we used \eqref{eq:uniform-continuity} in the last step.
    Moreover, in the $w$-coordinate, we have $g^{n+1}(w_n) \in D(0,\delta_g)$ for all $n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$, and consequently, $\norm{F^{n+1}(0,w_n)}$ is uniformly bounded over $n \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$.
    
    Assume that $(0,0)$ lies in the Fatou set of $F$.
    The iterates diverge at $(0,0)$, so any limit along a subsequence is $\infty$. 
    Normality then yields locally uniform divergence on a neighborhood of $(0,0)$, which contradicts $\norm{F^{n+1}(0,w_n)}$ being bounded while $(0,w_n) \to (0,0)$ as $n \to\infty$.
    Therefore, $(0,0)$ lies in the Julia set of $F$.

    Next, we prove that $\mathring{K_0} \times \set{0} \subset \mathcal{F}(F)$.
    We use induction to show that 
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:partial-wandering-induction}
        \pi_z F^n(z,w) \in K_n + 2n\pi i
    \end{equation}
    for all $n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$ and $(z,w) \in K_0 \times D(0,\delta_g)$. 
    
    The base case, $n = 0$, is trivial.
    Assume \eqref{eq:partial-wandering-induction} holds for some $n \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$. 
    Fix $(z,w) \in K_0 \times D(0,\delta_g)$.
    It is clear that $g^n(w) \in D(0,\delta_g)$. 
    By the induction hypothesis, it holds that $\pi_z F^n(z,w) - 2n\pi i \in K_n$, and therefore,
    \begin{equation*}
        \begin{split}
            f(\pi_z F^n(z,w)) &= f(\pi_z F^n(z,w) - 2 n\pi i) + 2 n\pi i \\
             
            &= b(\pi_z F^n(z,w) -  2 n\pi i) + (n + 1) 2 \pi i \in b(K_n) +  2 (n+1) \pi i.
        \end{split}
    \end{equation*}
    By \eqref{eq:partial-wandering-1}, we obtain
    \begin{equation*}
        \abs{\omega h(\zeta)} = \abs{\omega}\abs{h(\zeta)} \leq \abs{\omega} \varepsilon_{n} \leq \varepsilon_{n}
    \end{equation*}
    for all $(\zeta,\omega) \in ( K_n + 2n\pi i) \times D(0,\delta_g)$. Consequently,
    \begin{equation*}
        \begin{split}
            \pi_z F^{n+1}(z,w) &= f(\pi_z F^n(z,w)) + g^n(w) h(\pi_z F^n(z,w)) \\
             
            &\in b(K_n) +  2 (n+1)\pi i + \overline{D(0,\varepsilon_{n})} \\
             
            &\subset K_{n+1} +  2 (n+1)\pi i.
        \end{split}
    \end{equation*}
    This completes the induction.  

    Since $\min \abs{K_n + 2n \pi i} \to \infty$ as $n \to \infty$, we conclude from \eqref{eq:partial-wandering-induction} that the orbits of any points in $K_0 \times D(0,\delta_g)$ diverge uniformly to $\infty$ in the $z$-coordinate and converge uniformly to $0$ in the $w$-coordinate.
    Thus, it follows that $\mathring{K_0} \times D(0,\delta_g) \subset \mathcal{F}(F)$.
    Since $(0,0) \in \mathcal{J}(F)$ while $\mathring{K_0} \times \set{0} \subset \mathcal{F}(F)$, we have shown that the wandering domain $U$ is partially bulging.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- theorem-8: body_md -->
\label{thm:partial-baker}
    Let $f(z) \coloneqq z + 1 + e^{-z}$ and let $g$ be a nonconstant entire function with an attracting fixed point at $0$.
    Then there exists an entire function $h$ such that the skew product $F(z,w) = (f(z) + w h(z), g(w))$ has a partially bulging Baker domain. 
    More precisely, there exist two sequences $(S_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ and $(T_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of infinite vertical strips, i.e., sets of the form $\set{ z \in \mathbb{C} \mid a \leq \operatorname{Re}(z) \leq b}$ for $a < b$, which are contained in the Baker domain and satisfy
    \begin{itemize}
        \item $f(T_k) \subset T_{k+1}$ and $f(S_k) \subset S_{k+1}$ for all $k \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$,
        \item $T_0 \times \set{0} \subset \mathcal{J}(F)$, and
        \item $\bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}} \mathring{S_k} \times \set{0} \subset \mathcal{F}(F)$.
    \end{itemize}
<!-- end theorem-8 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
First, given two infinite vertical strips $S$ and $T$, we define
    \begin{equation*}
        [S,T] \coloneqq \set{z \in \mathbb{C} \mid \min_{s\in S}\operatorname{Re}(s) \leq \operatorname{Re}(z) \leq \max_{t\in T}\operatorname{Re}(t)}.
    \end{equation*}
    Furthermore, we write $S < T$ if $\max_{s\in S}\operatorname{Re}(s) < \min_{t\in T}\operatorname{Re}(t)$.

    It is well known from one-dimensional dynamics that $f$ has a Baker domain $B$ which has $\set{z \in \mathbb{C}\mid \operatorname{Re}(z) \geq R}$ as an absorbing domain (see \Cref{def:absorbing-domain}) for every $R > 0$.
    The right half-plane contains $f$-invariant sequences of infinite vertical strips as detailed in the following claim and shown in Figure~\ref{fig:partial-baker-strips}.
    \begin{claim}\label{claim:partial-baker}
        There exist three sequences $(S_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$, $(T_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$, and $(I_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of infinite vertical strips in $\set{z \in \mathbb{C} \mid \operatorname{Re} z > 0}$, a sequence of positive real numbers $(\varepsilon_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$, and $\delta > 0$, such that for all $k \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$,
        \begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
            \item $S_k < I_k < S_{k+1}$,\label{prop:partial-baker-2}
            \item $T_k + D(0,\delta) \subset I_k$,\label{prop:partial-baker-3}
            \item $f(I_k) \subset I_{k+1}$,\label{prop:partial-baker-4}
            \item $f(T_k) \subset T_{k+1}$, and \label{prop:partial-baker-5}
            \item $f(S_k) + D(0,\varepsilon_k) \subset S_{k+1}$. \label{prop:partial-baker-6}
        \end{enumerate}
    \end{claim}

    \begin{figure}[ht]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[x=2.6cm, y=1cm]
            \def0.6{0.6}                
            \def2{2}                                

                            
            \draw[->] (0,-2-0.3) -- (0,2+0.3) node[above] {$\operatorname{Im} z$};

            \foreach \k in {0,1,2}{
                \pgfmathsetmacro{\ws}{0.07+0.025*\k}                     
                \pgfmathsetmacro{\wt}{0.06+0.02*\k}                      
                \pgfmathsetmacro{\wi}{\wt+0.07}                                          
                \pgfmathsetmacro{\cs}{0.6+\k}                         
                \pgfmathsetmacro{\ct}{0.6+\k+0.5}                             

                     
                \fill[gray!10] (\ct-\wi,-2) rectangle (\ct+\wi,2);
                \draw (\ct-\wi,-2) -- (\ct-\wi,2);
                \draw (\ct+\wi,-2) -- (\ct+\wi,2);

                     
                \fill[gray!45] (\ct-\wt,-2) rectangle (\ct+\wt,2);

                     
                \fill[gray!45] (\cs-\ws,-2) rectangle (\cs+\ws,2);
                \draw (\cs-\ws,-2) -- (\cs-\ws,2);
                \draw (\cs+\ws,-2) -- (\cs+\ws,2);

                        
                \node[below] at (\cs,-2) {$S_{\k}$};
                \node[below] at (\ct,-2) {$T_{\k}$};
                \node[above] at (\ct,2)  {$I_{\k}$};
            }

                                                          
            \foreach \k in {0,1}{
                \pgfmathsetmacro{\cs}{0.6+\k}
                \pgfmathsetmacro{\ct}{0.6+\k+0.5}
                \draw[->, >=stealth] (\cs+0.1,2+0.3) to[bend left=30]
                    node[above] {$f$} (\cs+0.9,2+0.3);
                \draw[->, >=stealth] (\ct+0.1,-2-0.3) to[bend right=30]
                    node[below] {$f$} (\ct+0.9,-2-0.3);
            }

                       
            \draw[->] (-0.2,0) -- (0.6+2.9,0) node[right] {$\operatorname{Re} z$};
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{The strips from Claim~\ref{claim:partial-baker} for $k = 0,1,2$, cut off at the top and bottom.
        The function $f$ maps $S_k$ into $S_{k+1}$ and $T_k$ into $T_{k+1}$.}
        \label{fig:partial-baker-strips}
        \end{figure}
    \begin{subproof}
        Fix $\delta \coloneqq \frac{1}{16}$.
        Let $a > 0$ be such that $\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{j}{2} - a} < \delta$ and define $d_k \coloneqq \sum_{j=0}^{k}e^{-\frac{j}{2} - a}$.
        Let $(\varepsilon_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ be any sequence of positive real numbers with $\sum_{j=0}^{\infty} \varepsilon_j < \delta$ and define $e_k \coloneqq \sum_{j=0}^{k-1 } \varepsilon_j$.

        Define the vertical strips
        \begin{equation*}
            S_k \coloneqq \set*{ z \in \mathbb{C} \,\middle|\, a + k - d_k - e_k \leq \operatorname{Re}(z) \leq a + k + d_k + e_k },
        \end{equation*}
        \begin{equation*}
            I_k \coloneqq \set*{ z \in \mathbb{C} \,\middle|\, a + k + \frac{1}{2} - d_k - \delta \leq \operatorname{Re}(z) \leq a + k + \frac{1}{2} + d_k + \delta },
        \end{equation*}
        and
        \begin{equation*}
            T_k \coloneqq \set*{ z \in \mathbb{C} \,\middle|\, a + k + \frac{1}{2} - d_k \leq \operatorname{Re}(z) \leq a + k + \frac{1}{2} + d_k }.
        \end{equation*}
        By definition, these strips satisfy properties~\ref{prop:partial-baker-2} and \ref{prop:partial-baker-3}.
        Next, for $k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$ sufficiently large and $z \in S_k$,
        \begin{equation*}
            \begin{split}
                \operatorname{Re}(f(z)) &= \operatorname{Re}(z) + 1 + \operatorname{Re}(e^{-z}) \\
                 
                &\leq a + k + d_k + e_k + 1 + e^{-k - a + d_k + e_k} < a + (k+1) + d_{k+1} + e_k,
            \end{split}
        \end{equation*}
        and similarly, $\operatorname{Re}(f(z)) > a + (k+1) - d_{k+1} - e_k$.
        Thus, property~\ref{prop:partial-baker-6} follows for $k$ large enough.
        Properties~\ref{prop:partial-baker-4} and \ref{prop:partial-baker-5} can be deduced analogously. 
        Up to reindexing, the claim follows.
    \end{subproof}
    \textit{Proof of Theorem~{\ref{thm:partial-baker}} (continued).}
    Observe that all the strips defined in \Cref{claim:partial-baker} lie in $\set{z \in \mathbb{C}\mid \operatorname{Re}(z) \geq R}$ for some $R > 0$, and hence, in an absorbing domain of $B$.
    Let $Q = \set{q_j\mid j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ be a countable dense subset of $T_0$ with pairwise disjoint orbits.
    No point in $Q$ is preperiodic for $f$ since orbits escape to infinity.
    The goal is to apply a construction similar to the proof of \Cref{thm:nowhere-bulging} for this set $Q$ while maintaining good control on the strips $S_k$.

    To that end, we change a few steps of the construction as follows.
    Similarly to \Cref{claim:nowhere-bulging}, but with $n_k = k$, there exists a sequence $(\rho_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of positive real numbers such that letting $H_k \coloneqq [-S_{k-1}, S_{k-1}] \cup I_{k-1}$ for $k\geq 1$, where $-S_{k-1} \coloneqq \set{-z \mid z \in S_{k-1}}$, $H_0 = \emptyset$, and $\Delta^j_{m,k} \coloneqq \overline{D(f^j(q_m), \rho_k)}$, the following hold:
    \begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
        \item $\Delta^j_{m,k} \subset I_j$ for all $j,m,k \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$; in particular, $\Delta^j_{m,k} \subset H_k$ for all $j \in \set{0, \dots, k-1}$ and $m,k \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$,
        \item for every $k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$, all the sets $\Delta^j_{m,k}$, $m = 0,\dots,k$, $j = 0, \dots, k$, are pairwise disjoint,
        \item for every $k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$,
        \begin{equation*}
            H_k \cup \bigcup_{m = 0}^k \Delta^k_{m,k} \subset H_{k+1},
        \end{equation*}
        \item $H_k$ and $ \Delta^{k}_{0,k} \cup \dots \cup \Delta^{k}_{k,k}$ are disjoint, and\label{prop:partial-baker-7}
        \item for all $z, z' \in \Delta^j_{m,k}$, $k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$, $m = 0,\dots,k$, and $j = 0, \dots, k$,
        \begin{equation*}
            \abs{f(z) - f(z')} < \frac{1}{4}.
        \end{equation*}
    \end{enumerate}
    Observe, as before, that $H_k \subset H_{k+1}$ and $\mathbb{C} = \bigcup_{k \in \mathbb{N}_{0}} H_k$.
    Furthermore, $H_k$ is disjoint from the sets $ \Delta^{k}_{0,k}, \dots, \Delta^{k}_{k,k}$ by property~\ref{prop:partial-baker-7}.
    In this construction, the set $H_k$ no longer has connected complement in $\mathbb{C}$ but, as the disjoint union of two infinite vertical strips, it satisfies the conditions of Arakelyan's Theorem~\ref{thm:arakelyan}.

    We then proceed to prove a variant of \Cref{lemma:nowhere-bulging-2} with property~\ref{prop:nowhere-bulging-lemma-2-6} changed to
    \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*$'$), start=7]
        \item $\norm{h_k - h_{k-1}}_{H_k} < \frac{1}{2^{k+1}} \min\set{\delta_0, \dots, \delta_k, \varepsilon_0, \dots, \varepsilon_k}$ \label{prop:partial-baker-8}
    \end{enumerate}
    with the sequence $(\varepsilon_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ from \Cref{claim:partial-baker}, and additionally requiring that
    \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*), start=9]
        \item $\norm*{h_k}_{S_k} < \frac{\varepsilon_k}{2}$.\label{prop:partial-baker-9}
    \end{enumerate}
    The original proof of \Cref{lemma:nowhere-bulging-2} stays almost unchanged.
    First, we start the construction with $h_0 \coloneqq c e^z$ instead of $\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{C}}$ with a constant $c > 0$ small enough that $\norm*{h_0}_{S_0} < \frac{\varepsilon_0}{2}$.
    Next, observe that $K \coloneqq H_{k+1} \cup \bigcup_{m = 0}^{k+1} \paren*{A_m^{k+1} \cup B_m^{k+1}} \cup S_{k+1}$, with $A_m^{k+1} $ and $ B_m^{k+1}$ as in \Cref{lemma:nowhere-bulging-2}, satisfies all conditions of Arakelyan's Theorem~\ref{thm:arakelyan}.
    Then, the above variant of \Cref{lemma:nowhere-bulging-2} is achieved by adapting the recursive construction of $h_{k+1}$ to approximate the function $\Phi \colon K \to \mathbb{C}$,
    \begin{equation*}
        \Phi(z) \coloneqq
        \begin{cases}
            h_k(z) & \text{if } z \in H_{k+1}, \\
            -\frac{1}{g^{k + 1}(w_{k+1})} f(\zeta_{k+1, m}) & \text{if } z \in A_m^{k+1} \text{ for some } m, \\
            -\frac{1}{g^{k + 1}(w'_{k+1})} (f(\zeta'_{k+1, m}) - (k+1)) & \text{if } z \in B_m^{k+1}\text{ for some } m, \\
            0 & \text{if } z \in S_{k+1},
        \end{cases}
    \end{equation*}
    by an entire function up to an error of $\frac{1}{2^{k+2}}\min\set{\delta_0, \dots, \delta_{k+1}, \varepsilon_0, \dots, \varepsilon_{k+1}}$.
    The rest of the proof stays unchanged.
    The construction then yields an entire function $h$ such that $T_0 \times \set{0} \subset \mathcal{J}(F)$ where $F(z,w)\coloneqq(f(z) + wh(z), g(w))$.
    Furthermore, analogously to \eqref{eq:nowhere-bulging-9}, since $S_k \subset H_{k+1}$, it follows from property~\ref{prop:partial-baker-8} that
    \begin{equation*}
        \norm{h - h_k}_{S_k} \leq \sum_{j = k+1}^{\infty} 2^{-(j+1)} \min\set{\delta_0, \dots, \delta_j, \varepsilon_0, \dots, \varepsilon_j} \leq \sum_{j = k+1}^{\infty} 2^{-(j+1)} \varepsilon_k \leq \frac{\varepsilon_k}{2},
    \end{equation*}
    and together with \ref{prop:partial-baker-9}, it follows that
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:small-on-s}
        \norm{h}_{S_k} < \varepsilon_k
    \end{equation}
    for all $k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$.
    We show that $\bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}} \mathring{S_k} \times \set{0}\subset \mathcal{F}(F)$.
    Since $0$ is an attracting fixed point of $g$, there exist $0 < \alpha < 1$ and $0 < \delta_g < 1$ such that
    \begin{equation*}
        \abs{g(w)} \leq \alpha \abs{w}
    \end{equation*} 
    for all $w \in D(0,\delta_g)$.
    Set $A \coloneqq D(0,\delta_g)$. 
    Then $A$ is a $g$-invariant neighborhood of $0$ such that $g^k(w) \to 0$ as $k \to \infty$ uniformly for all $w \in A$.
    By \eqref{eq:small-on-s} and property~\ref{prop:partial-baker-6} from \Cref{claim:partial-baker}, it follows that for all $k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$ and for all $(z,w) \in S_k \times A$,
    \begin{equation*}
        \pi_z F(z,w) = f(z) + wh(z) \in f(S_k) + wD(0,\varepsilon_k) \subset f(S_k) + D(0,\varepsilon_k) \subset S_{k+1}. 
    \end{equation*}
    Since $\min_{s\in S_k}\operatorname{Re}(s) \to \infty$ as $k \to \infty$, points in $S_k \times A$ diverge uniformly to $\infty$ in the $z$-coordinate and converge uniformly to $0$ in the $w$-coordinate. 
    We conclude that $\mathring{S_k} \times \set{0} \subset \mathcal{F}(F)$.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- claim-4: body_md -->
\label{claim:partial-baker}
        There exist three sequences $(S_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$, $(T_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$, and $(I_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of infinite vertical strips in $\set{z \in \mathbb{C} \mid \operatorname{Re} z > 0}$, a sequence of positive real numbers $(\varepsilon_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$, and $\delta > 0$, such that for all $k \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$,
        \begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
            \item $S_k < I_k < S_{k+1}$,\label{prop:partial-baker-2}
            \item $T_k + D(0,\delta) \subset I_k$,\label{prop:partial-baker-3}
            \item $f(I_k) \subset I_{k+1}$,\label{prop:partial-baker-4}
            \item $f(T_k) \subset T_{k+1}$, and \label{prop:partial-baker-5}
            \item $f(S_k) + D(0,\varepsilon_k) \subset S_{k+1}$. \label{prop:partial-baker-6}
        \end{enumerate}
<!-- end claim-4 -->
<!-- subproof-4: body_md -->
ix $\delta \coloneqq \frac{1}{16}$.
        Let $a > 0$ be such that $\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{j}{2} - a} < \delta$ and define $d_k \coloneqq \sum_{j=0}^{k}e^{-\frac{j}{2} - a}$.
        Let $(\varepsilon_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ be any sequence of positive real numbers with $\sum_{j=0}^{\infty} \varepsilon_j < \delta$ and define $e_k \coloneqq \sum_{j=0}^{k-1 } \varepsilon_j$.

        Define the vertical strips
        \begin{equation*}
            S_k \coloneqq \set*{ z \in \mathbb{C} \,\middle|\, a + k - d_k - e_k \leq \operatorname{Re}(z) \leq a + k + d_k + e_k },
        \end{equation*}
        \begin{equation*}
            I_k \coloneqq \set*{ z \in \mathbb{C} \,\middle|\, a + k + \frac{1}{2} - d_k - \delta \leq \operatorname{Re}(z) \leq a + k + \frac{1}{2} + d_k + \delta },
        \end{equation*}
        and
        \begin{equation*}
            T_k \coloneqq \set*{ z \in \mathbb{C} \,\middle|\, a + k + \frac{1}{2} - d_k \leq \operatorname{Re}(z) \leq a + k + \frac{1}{2} + d_k }.
        \end{equation*}
        By definition, these strips satisfy properties~\ref{prop:partial-baker-2} and \ref{prop:partial-baker-3}.
        Next, for $k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$ sufficiently large and $z \in S_k$,
        \begin{equation*}
            \begin{split}
                \operatorname{Re}(f(z)) &= \operatorname{Re}(z) + 1 + \operatorname{Re}(e^{-z}) \\
                 
                &\leq a + k + d_k + e_k + 1 + e^{-k - a + d_k + e_k} < a + (k+1) + d_{k+1} + e_k,
            \end{split}
        \end{equation*}
        and similarly, $\operatorname{Re}(f(z)) > a + (k+1) - d_{k+1} - e_k$.
        Thus, property~\ref{prop:partial-baker-6} follows for $k$ large enough.
        Properties~\ref{prop:partial-baker-4} and \ref{prop:partial-baker-5} can be deduced analogously. 
        Up to reindexing, the claim follows.
<!-- end subproof-4 -->
<!-- theorem-9: body_md -->
\label{thm:tame-perturbation}
	Let $(V_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ and $(W_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ be sequences of Jordan domains in $\mathbb{C}$ such that 
	\begin{itemize}
		\item the sets $\overline{W_n}$ are pairwise disjoint,
		\item $\overline{V_n} \subset W_n$, and
		\item $\min \abs{\overline{W_n}} \to \infty$ as $n\to\infty$.
	\end{itemize}
	Let $r \colon \mathbb{C}^2 \to \mathbb{C}$ be a nonconstant holomorphic function that vanishes on $\set{w=0}$ and let $g$ be a nonconstant entire function with an attracting fixed point at $0$.
	Then there exists an entire function $f$ with a wandering domain $U$ such that the Fatou components $U_n$ containing $f^n(U)$ satisfy $\overline{V_n} \subset U_n \subset W_n$ for all $n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$ and such that the wandering domain $U$ is non-bulging for the skew product $F(z,w) = (f(z) + r(z,w), g(w))$.
<!-- end theorem-9 -->
<!-- definition-9: body_md -->
We say that a curve $\sigma$ in $\mathbb{C}$ \emph{surrounds a set $B\subset \mathbb{C}$} if $B$ is contained in a bounded component of the complement of $\sigma$. For a Jordan curve $\gamma$ we denote by $\operatorname{int} \gamma$ the bounded component of $\mathbb{C} \setminus \gamma$ and by $\operatorname{ext} \gamma$ the unbounded component.
<!-- end definition-9 -->
<!-- definition-10: body_md -->
Given $\varepsilon > 0$ and curves $\gamma\colon[0,1]\to\mathbb{C}$ and $\eta\colon[0,1]\to\mathbb{C}$, we say that $\gamma$ and $\eta$ are \emph{$\varepsilon$-close to each other}, written $\gamma \sim_\varepsilon \eta$, if their supports are $\varepsilon$-close to each other, i.e., $\gamma([0,1]) \subset \eta([0,1]) + D(0,\varepsilon)$ and $\eta([0,1]) \subset \gamma([0,1]) + D(0,\varepsilon)$.
<!-- end definition-10 -->
<!-- theorem-10: body_md -->
\label{thm:theorem-D}
    Let $f$ be a transcendental entire function and suppose that there exist Jordan curves $\gamma_n$ and $\Gamma_n$, $n\ge 0$, a bounded domain $D$, a subsequence $n_k\to\infty$ and  compact sets $L_k$ (associated with $\Gamma_{n_k}$) such that
    \begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
        \item $\Gamma_n$ surrounds $\gamma_n$, for $n \geq 0$, \label{prop:theorem-D-1}
        \item  for every $k,n, m\geq0$, $m\neq n$ the sets $L_k, \overline{D}, \Gamma_m$  are in $\operatorname{ext}\Gamma_n$, \label{prop:theorem-D-2}
        \item  $\gamma_{n+1}$ surrounds $f(\gamma_n)$, for $n \geq 0$, \label{prop:theorem-D-3}
        \item $f(\Gamma_n)$ surrounds $\Gamma_{n+1}$, for $n \geq 0$, \label{prop:theorem-D-4}
        \item $f(\overline D \cup \bigcup_{k\ge 0} L_k)\subset D$, and \label{prop:theorem-D-5}
        \item$ \max\{\operatorname{dist}(z,L_{k})\mid z \in \Gamma_{n_k}\} = o(\operatorname{dist}(\gamma_{n_k}, \Gamma_{n_k}))\;\text{ as}\; k \to \infty$. \label{prop:theorem-D-6}
    \end{enumerate}
    Then there exists an orbit of simply connected wandering domains $U_n$
    such that $\overline{\operatorname{int} \gamma_n} \subset U_n \subset \operatorname{int}\Gamma_n$, for $n \geq 0$.
<!-- end theorem-10 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lemma:outside-jordan}
	Let $\Omega \subset \mathbb{C}$ be a Jordan domain and $v \in \mathbb{C}\setminus\set{0}$. 
    Then there exists $x \in \partial\Omega$ with
	\begin{equation*}
		\overline{\Omega} \cap \mathbb{H}(x, v) = \emptyset
	\end{equation*}
	where
	\begin{equation*}
		\mathbb{H}(x, v) \coloneqq \set{ x + sv + tv^{\perp } \mid s > 0 \text{ and } t \in \mathbb{R}}
	\end{equation*}
	for any vector $v^{\perp} \in \mathbb{C}\setminus\set{0}$ perpendicular to $v$.
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
Consider the lines
	\begin{equation*}
		L_s \coloneqq \set{ sv + tv^{\perp}  \mid t \in \mathbb{R} }
	\end{equation*}
	for $s \in \mathbb{R}$. Since $\Omega$ is nonempty, the set of intersections
	\begin{equation*}
		I \coloneqq \set{ s \in \mathbb{R} \mid L_s \cap \overline{\Omega} \neq \emptyset }
	\end{equation*}
	is nonempty. 
    The set $I$, being the projection of the compact set $\overline\Omega$ onto the $v$-direction, is compact.
    Let $s \coloneqq \max I$. 
    Any $x \in L_s \cap \overline{\Omega}$ lies in $\partial \Omega$ and suffices for the statement.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
This proof is a variant of the construction in \cite[Theorem 5.3]{Benini2022} and, in particular, parts proceed similarly.
    The idea is to construct wandering domains of a prescribed shape that simultaneously contain points near their boundary which, upon adding the given perturbation, are pushed into a region that $f$ maps close to the origin.

	First, let $\delta_g > 0$ be such that $\abs{g(w)} < \abs{w}$ and $g(w) \neq 0$ for all $w \in D(0,\delta_g)\setminus\set{0}$.
    Furthermore, we can write $r(z,w) = w^d h(z,w)$, $d\geq 1$, where $h$ is a holomorphic function that does not vanish identically on $\set{w=0}$.

	The key step of the proof is accomplished in the following lemma.

	\begin{lemma}\label{lemma:tame-perturbation-1}
		There exist an entire function $f$ with a wandering domain $U$, a sequence $(\zeta_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of complex numbers, a sequence $(\rho_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of positive real numbers, a sequence $(w_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of nonzero complex numbers converging to $0$, and a sequence of compact simply connected sets $(K_n)_{n \geq 1}$ such that for all $n \geq 0$,
		\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
			\item $\abs{w_n} < \delta_g$, \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-1}
			\item the Fatou component $U_n$ of $f$ containing $f^n(U)$ satisfies $\overline{V_n} \subset U_n \subset W_n$, \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-4}
			\item $f(z) + g^{n}(w_n)^d h(z, g^{n}(w_n)) \in K_{n+1}$ for all $ z \in \overline{D(\zeta_{n}, 3\rho_n)} $, \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-5}
			\item $f(z) + g^{n}(w_j)^d h(z, g^{n}(w_j)) \in \overline{D(\zeta_{n+1}, 3\rho_{n+1})}$ for all $z \in \overline{D(\zeta_n, 3\rho_n)}$ and $j >  n$, \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-6}
			\item $f(z) + g^{n+1}(w_n)^d h(z,g^{n+1}(w_n)) \in \mathbb{D} $ for all $z \in K_{n+1}$, and \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-7}
			\item $\zeta_n \in V_n$. \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-extra}
		\end{enumerate}
	\end{lemma}

	\textit{Proof of Theorem~{\ref{thm:tame-perturbation}} (continued).}
    In \Cref{lemma:tame-perturbation-1}, property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-4} yields the sequence of wandering domains, whereas properties~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-5} and \ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-6} ensure the existence of points sufficiently close to the boundary to be pushed outside by the perturbation.
    Property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-7} forces these points to be mapped close to the origin in the next iteration. 

	Using \Cref{lemma:tame-perturbation-1}, we now proceed to show that the wandering domain $U$ of $f$ is non-bulging for
	\begin{equation*}
		F(z,w) = (f(z) + r(z,w), g(w)) = (f(z) + w^d h(z,w), g(w)).
	\end{equation*}
	We first show that
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:tame-perturbation-1}
		\pi_z F^{n+2}(\zeta_0, w_n) \in \mathbb{D}
	\end{equation}
	for all $n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$. 
    Indeed, property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-6} applied inductively yields
	\begin{equation*}
		\pi_z F^{n}(\zeta_0, w_n) \in \overline{D(\zeta_n, 3 \rho_n)}.
	\end{equation*}
	Then, with property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-5},
	\begin{equation*}
		\begin{split}
			\pi_z F^{n+1}(\zeta_0, w_n) &= f(\pi_z F^{n}(\zeta_0, w_n)) + g^n(w_n)^d h(\pi_z F^{n}(\zeta_0, w_n), g^n(w_n)) \in K_{n+1}.
		\end{split}
	\end{equation*}
	Finally, property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-7} implies
	\begin{equation*}
		\pi_z F^{n+2}(\zeta_0, w_n) \in \mathbb{D}.
	\end{equation*}
	
    Assume that $U$ is bulging.
    Then the iterates of $F$ form a normal family around $(\zeta_0, 0)$ since $\zeta_0 \in V_0 \subset U$ by \ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-4} and \ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-extra}. 
    In particular, there exist a compact neighborhood $K$ of $(\zeta_0, 0)$ and a subsequence $(F^{n_k + 2})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of iterates such that this subsequence either diverges uniformly to infinity on $K$ or converges uniformly to a finite limit map. 
	By property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-4}, we have $f^n(U) \subset W_n$ and therefore, $\min\abs{W_n} \to \infty$ implies $f^n(z) \to \infty$ as $n\to \infty$ for all $z \in U$.
    Thus, $(F^{n_k + 2})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ has to diverge uniformly to infinity on $K$.
    In particular, there exists $J \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$ with
    \begin{equation*}
        \norm{F^{n_{k} + 2}(z,w)} \geq 2
    \end{equation*}
    for all $k \geq J$ and all $(z,w) \in K$.
	Also, there exists $J'\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$ such that $(\zeta_0, w_{n_k}) \in K$ for all $k \geq J'$ since $\lim_{k\to \infty} w_k = 0$.
	Lastly, let $J'' \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$ be such that for all $k \geq J''$, we have $\abs{g^{n_k + 2}(w_{n_k})} \leq \abs{w_{n_k}} < 1$.
	Then, together with \eqref{eq:tame-perturbation-1}, we obtain for all $k \geq \max\set{J, J', J''}$,
	\begin{equation*}
		2 \leq \norm{F^{n_{k} + 2}(\zeta_0,w_{n_k})} \leq \abs{\pi_z F^{n_{k} + 2}(\zeta_0,w_{n_k})} + \abs{g^{n_k + 2}(w_{n_k})} < 1 + 1 = 2,
	\end{equation*}
	which is a contradiction. Hence, $U$ cannot be bulging.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{lemma:tame-perturbation-1}
		There exist an entire function $f$ with a wandering domain $U$, a sequence $(\zeta_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of complex numbers, a sequence $(\rho_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of positive real numbers, a sequence $(w_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of nonzero complex numbers converging to $0$, and a sequence of compact simply connected sets $(K_n)_{n \geq 1}$ such that for all $n \geq 0$,
		\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
			\item $\abs{w_n} < \delta_g$, \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-1}
			\item the Fatou component $U_n$ of $f$ containing $f^n(U)$ satisfies $\overline{V_n} \subset U_n \subset W_n$, \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-4}
			\item $f(z) + g^{n}(w_n)^d h(z, g^{n}(w_n)) \in K_{n+1}$ for all $ z \in \overline{D(\zeta_{n}, 3\rho_n)} $, \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-5}
			\item $f(z) + g^{n}(w_j)^d h(z, g^{n}(w_j)) \in \overline{D(\zeta_{n+1}, 3\rho_{n+1})}$ for all $z \in \overline{D(\zeta_n, 3\rho_n)}$ and $j >  n$, \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-6}
			\item $f(z) + g^{n+1}(w_n)^d h(z,g^{n+1}(w_n)) \in \mathbb{D} $ for all $z \in K_{n+1}$, and \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-7}
			\item $\zeta_n \in V_n$. \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-extra}
		\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
To construct the function $f$, we need several sequences of constants as provided by the following lemma. 
	\begin{lemma}\label{lemma:tame-perturbation-2}
		There exist sequences $(\zeta_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of complex numbers, $(\rho_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of positive real numbers, and $(w_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of nonzero complex numbers such that for all $n \geq 0$,
		\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
			\item $\abs{w_n} < \min\set*{\delta_g, \frac{1}{n+1}}$, \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-1}
			\item $\overline{D(\zeta_n, 3\rho_n)} \subset V_n$,\label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-2}
			\item $\overline{D(\zeta_{n+1}, 3\rho_{n+1})} + g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\overline{D(\zeta_n, 3 \rho_n)}, g^{n}(w_n)) \subset \paren*{W_{n+1} \setminus \overline{V_{n+1}}} \cap \mathbb H_{n+1}$, for an open half-plane $\mathbb H_{n+1}$ with $\mathbb H_{n+1} \cap \overline{V_{n+1}} = \emptyset$,\label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-3}
			\item $\abs{g^{k}(w_n)}^d \max_{z \in \overline{D(\zeta_k, 3\rho_k)}, w \in \overline{\mathbb{D}}} \abs{h(z,w)} < \rho_{k+1}$ for all $k \in \set{0,\dots, n-1}$, \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-4}
			\item $h(\overline{D(\zeta_n, 3\rho_n)}, \overline{D(0,\abs{g^n(w_n)})}) \subset \mathbb{H}\paren*{\frac{h(\zeta_n, 0)}{2},h(\zeta_n, 0)}$, \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-5}  
			\item $h(\zeta_n, 0) \neq 0$, \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-6}  
			\item $\abs{g^n(w_n)}^d \sup \abs{h(\overline{D(\zeta_n, 3\rho_n)}, \overline{D(0,\abs{g^n(w_n)})})} < \operatorname{dist}(\partial V_{n+1}, \partial W_{n+1})$, and \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-7}  
			\item $ \abs{g^n(w_n)}^d \sup \abs{h(\overline{W_{n+1}}, \overline{\mathbb{D}})} < \frac{1}{2} $. \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-8} 
		\end{enumerate}
	\end{lemma}
	\begin{proof}[Proof of Lemma~{\ref{lemma:tame-perturbation-2}}]
		The sequences are constructed inductively.

		Choose any $\zeta_0 \in V_0$ such that $h(\zeta_0, 0) \neq 0$; hence, property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-6} holds. 
        Such an element exists since $z \mapsto h(z, 0)$ is not identically zero. 
        By continuity, there exist $\rho_0 > 0$ and $w_0 \in \mathbb{C}\setminus\set{0}$ such that $h(\overline{D(\zeta_0, 3\rho_0)}, \overline{D(0,\abs{w_0})}) \subset \mathbb{H}(\frac{h(\zeta_0, 0)}{2},h(\zeta_0, 0))$, i.e., property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-5} holds. 
        Further shrinking $\rho_0$ guarantees that $\overline{D(\zeta_0, 3\rho_0)} \subset V_0$, yielding property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-2}, and further shrinking $w_0$, we obtain properties~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-1}, \ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-7}, and \ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-8}.

        Suppose $\zeta_0, \dots, \zeta_n$, $\rho_0, \dots, \rho_n$, and $w_0, \dots, w_n$ have been constructed satisfying properties~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-1} through \ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-8}.
        We construct the next elements as follows.

		Using property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-5}, we first obtain
		\begin{equation*}
			g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\overline{D(\zeta_n, 3 \rho_n)}, g^{n}(w_n)) \subset \mathbb{H}\paren*{g^{n}(w_n)^d\frac{h(\zeta_n, 0)}{2},g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\zeta_n, 0)}.
		\end{equation*}
		Applying \Cref{lemma:outside-jordan} to the Jordan domain $V_{n+1}$ with $v \coloneqq g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\zeta_n, 0) \neq 0$ yields a point $x \in \partial V_{n+1}$ such that 
		\begin{equation*}
			\overline{V_{n+1}} \cap \mathbb{H}\paren*{x, g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\zeta_n, 0)} = \emptyset.
		\end{equation*}
        Set $\mathbb{H}_{n+1} \coloneqq \mathbb{H}\paren*{x, g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\zeta_n, 0)}$.
		Then, for any $z \in V_{n+1} \cap D\paren*{x, \abs*{\frac{g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\zeta_n, 0)}{4}}} \neq \emptyset$, we obtain
		\begin{equation*}
			z + g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\overline{D(\zeta_n, 3 \rho_n)}, g^{n}(w_n)) \subset \paren*{\mathbb{C} \setminus \overline{V_{n+1}}} \cap \mathbb H_{n+1}.
		\end{equation*}
		Choose any $\zeta_{n+1} \in V_{n+1} \cap D\paren*{x, \abs*{\frac{g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\zeta_n, 0)}{4}}}$ with $h(\zeta_{n+1}, 0) \neq 0$.
		Thus, property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-6} holds.
		Furthermore, let $R > 0$ be small enough so that
		\begin{equation*}
			\overline{D(\zeta_{n+1}, 3R)} \subset D\paren*{x, \abs*{\frac{g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\zeta_n, 0)}{4}}}.
		\end{equation*}
		We now show that property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-3} holds for all $\rho_{n+1} \leq R$. We have already proven that
		\begin{equation*}
			\overline{D(\zeta_{n+1}, 3\rho_{n+1})} + g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\overline{D(\zeta_n, 3 \rho_n)}, g^{n}(w_n)) \subset  \paren*{\mathbb{C} \setminus \overline{V_{n+1}}} \cap \mathbb H_{n+1}.
		\end{equation*}
		Using $\overline{D(\zeta_{n+1}, 3\rho_{n+1})} \subset V_{n+1}$ and property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-7} shows that
		\begin{equation*}
			\overline{D(\zeta_{n+1}, 3\rho_{n+1})} + g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\overline{D(\zeta_n, 3 \rho_n)}, g^{n}(w_n)) \subset W_{n+1}.
		\end{equation*}
		Taken together, these inclusions yield property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-3}.
		Lastly, we construct $\rho_{n+1}$ and $w_{n+1}$. By our choice of $\zeta_{n+1}$, we have $h(\zeta_{n+1},0)\neq 0$. By continuity, there exist $w_{n+1} \in \mathbb{C}\setminus\set{0}$ and $\rho_{n+1} > 0$ so small that
		\begin{equation*}
			h(\overline{D(\zeta_{n+1}, 3\rho_{n+1})}, \overline{D(0,\abs{g^{n+1}(w_{n+1})})}) \subset \mathbb{H}\paren*{\frac{h(\zeta_{n+1}, 0)}{2},h(\zeta_{n+1}, 0)}.
		\end{equation*}
		Without loss of generality, we may assume that $\rho_{n+1} \leq R$, yielding properties~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-3} and \ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-5}.
		Further shrinking $\rho_{n+1}$ yields property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-2}, and further shrinking $w_{n+1}$ yields properties~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-1}, \ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-4}, \ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-7}, and \ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-8}.
	\end{proof}
	\textit{Proof of Lemma~{\ref{lemma:tame-perturbation-1}} (continued).}
	We use \Cref{lemma:tame-perturbation-2}.
    Set $\delta_0 \coloneqq 1$ and define for $n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$,
	\begin{equation*}
		\delta_{n+1} \coloneqq \operatorname{dist}(\partial V_{n+1}, \overline{D(\zeta_{n+1}, 3\rho_{n+1})} + g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\overline{D(\zeta_n, 3 \rho_n)}, g^{n}(w_n))).
	\end{equation*}
	It follows from property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-3} and
    the compactness of
	\begin{equation*}
		\overline{D(\zeta_{n+1}, 3\rho_{n+1})} + g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\overline{D(\zeta_n, 3 \rho_n)}, g^{n}(w_n))
	\end{equation*}
    that $\delta_{n+1} > 0$.

    We will use \Cref{thm:theorem-D} to obtain wandering domains. 
	Let $\xi \in \mathbb{C} \setminus \bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}} \overline{W_k}$ and choose $r_{\xi} > 0$ so small that $D \coloneqq D(\xi, r_{\xi}) $ satisfies $\overline{D}\subset \mathbb{C} \setminus \bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}} \overline{W_k}$.

	Let $\gamma_n \coloneqq \partial V_n$. 
    Choose any Jordan curve $\Gamma_n$ completely inside $W_n \setminus \overline{V_n}$ such that $\gamma_n \sim_{\frac{\delta_n}{2}} \Gamma_n$.
    Moreover, it holds that $\overline{V_n} \subset \operatorname{int}\Gamma_n$.
    Fix any $0 < \alpha_n < \delta_n$ with $\operatorname{dist}(\partial W_n, \Gamma_n) > \alpha_n$ and $\operatorname{dist}(\partial V_n, \Gamma_n) > \alpha_n$.
	Let $W_n'$ be the Jordan domain enclosed by $\Gamma_n$, i.e., let $W_n' \coloneqq \operatorname{int}\Gamma_n$.

	Since $\Gamma_n$ is a Jordan curve, the set $\operatorname{ext}(\Gamma_n) \cap (\Gamma_n + D(0,\varepsilon))$ is path connected for every $\varepsilon > 0$. 
    Thus, for every $0 < \beta_n < \alpha_n$, we find a Jordan curve $\eta_n$ in $\operatorname{ext}(\Gamma_n) \cap W_n$ such that
	\begin{itemize}
		\item $\Gamma_n \subset \operatorname{int} \eta_n$, and
		\item $\Gamma_n \sim_{\frac{\beta_n}{2}} \eta_n$.
	\end{itemize}
    Since $\eta_n([0,1])$ is compact and lies in the open set $\operatorname{ext}(\Gamma_n) \cap W_n$, the curve $\eta_n$ has positive distance to $\Gamma_n$ and $\partial W_n$.
	We may assume that $\beta_n = o(\alpha_n)$ as $n \to \infty$, e.g., by choosing $\beta_n < \frac{\alpha_n}{n}$.

	Parametrizing $\eta_n$ as a curve defined on $[0,1]$, we find $0 < \ell_n < 1$ such that $\Gamma_n \sim_{\beta_n} \eta_n|_{[\ell_n, 1]}$.
    Let $L_n \coloneqq \eta_n([\ell_n, 1])$.
    Then,
    \begin{equation*}
        \max\set{\operatorname{dist}(z, L_n) \mid z \in \Gamma_n} < \beta_n = o(\alpha_n),
    \end{equation*}
    and observe further that the set $L_n$ is compact and simply connected.

	Consider the Riemann maps $\rho_{W_n'} \colon W_n' \to \mathbb{D}$ and $\rho_{W_n} \colon W_n \to \mathbb{D}$ with $\rho_{W_n'}(\zeta_n) = 0$ and $\rho_{W_n}(\zeta_n) = 0$. 
    By Carathéodory's Theorem for the continuation of a Riemann map to the boundary (see \cite[Theorem 6.19]{Zakeri2021}), both maps admit a bijective extension onto the boundary which we denote by the same symbols.

	Since $\rho_{W_n'}(\overline{V_n})$ is a compact subset of the open unit disk, for every $s > 0 $ there exists $d_n = d_n(s) \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$ large enough that $B_n \colon \overline{\mathbb{D}} \to \overline{\mathbb{D}}, z \mapsto z^{d_n} $ satisfies 
	\begin{equation*}
		B_n \circ \rho_{W_n'}(\overline{V_n}) \subset \overline{D(0,s)}.
	\end{equation*}
	In particular, since $\rho_{W_{n+1}}(D(\zeta_{n+1},\rho_{n+1}))$ is open and contains $0$, there exists $d_n \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$ such that
	\begin{equation*}
		B_n \circ \rho_{W_n'}(\overline{V_n}) \subset \rho_{W_{n+1}}(D(\zeta_{n+1},\rho_{n+1}))
	\end{equation*}
	or equivalently
	\begin{equation*}
		\rho_{W_{n+1}}^{-1} \circ B_n \circ \rho_{W_n'} (\overline{V_n}) \subset D(\zeta_{n+1},\rho_{n+1}).
	\end{equation*}
	Consider $\Phi_n \colon \overline{W_n'} \to \overline{W_{n+1}}$,
	\begin{equation*}
		\Phi_n(z) \coloneqq \rho_{W_{n+1}}^{-1} \circ B_n \circ \rho_{W_n'}(z).
	\end{equation*}
	Then $\Phi_n$ is continuous on $\overline{W_n'}$ and holomorphic on the interior. Furthermore,
	\begin{itemize}
		\item $\Phi_n(\overline{V_n}) \subset D(\zeta_{n+1},\rho_{n+1}) \subset \overline{D(\zeta_{n+1},2\rho_{n+1})} \subset V_{n+1}$,
		\item $\Phi_n(\Gamma_n) = \partial W_{n+1}$. In particular, $\Phi_n(\Gamma_n)$ surrounds $\Gamma_{n+1}$.
	\end{itemize}
	Choose $0 < \varepsilon_n < 1$ such that for every entire function $f$ the inequality $\norm{f - \Phi_n}_{\overline{W'_n}} < \varepsilon_n$ implies
	\begin{itemize}
		\item $f(\overline{V_n}) \subset \overline{D(\zeta_{n+1},2\rho_{n+1})}$, and
		\item $f(\Gamma_n) \subset \partial W_{n+1} + D(0,\beta_{n+1})$ and it surrounds $ \Gamma_{n+1} $.
	\end{itemize}
    From $\eta_{n+1} \sim_{\beta_{n+1}/2} \Gamma_{n+1}$ and $\Gamma_{n+1} \sim_{\delta_{n+1}/2} \gamma_{n+1}$, it follows that $\eta_{n+1} \subset \partial V_{n+1} + D(0,\delta_{n+1})$.
    Using property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-3} and the definition of $\delta_{n+1}$, we obtain
	\begin{equation*}
		\overline{D(\zeta_{n+1}, 3\rho_{n+1})} + g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\overline{D(\zeta_n, 3 \rho_n)}, g^{n}(w_n)) \subset \operatorname{ext}(\eta_{n+1}).
	\end{equation*}
	Furthermore, since this set is compact, all of its holes are bounded, and since it lies in the half-plane $\mathbb{H}_{n+1}$ outside of $V_{n+1}$, none of its holes can contain $\eta_{n+1}$. 
    Hence, the filled hull $\widetilde{K}_{n+1}$ of this set, i.e., its union with all bounded components of its complement, is a compact simply connected set with $\widetilde{K}_{n+1} \subset W_{n+1} \setminus \overline{\operatorname{int}\eta_{n+1}}$. 
    Since $\widetilde K_{n+1}$ is compact, we can choose $R_{n+1} > 0$ so small that $\widetilde{K}_{n+1} + \overline{D(0,R_{n+1})}$ still lies in the half-plane $\mathbb{H}_{n+1}$ and in $W_{n+1} \setminus \overline{\operatorname{int}\eta_{n+1}}$. 
    By the same argument as above, its filled hull $K_{n+1}$ then also satisfies $K_{n+1} \subset W_{n+1} \setminus \overline{\operatorname{int}\eta_{n+1}}$.
	Let
	\begin{equation*}
		E \coloneqq \overline{D} \cup \bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}} L_n \cup \bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}} \overline{W'_n} \cup \bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}} K_n.
	\end{equation*}
	This set satisfies all conditions of Nersesjan's Theorem~\ref{thm:nersesjan}.
	Define $\Psi \colon E \to \mathbb{C}$,
	\begin{equation*}
		\Psi(z) \coloneqq \begin{cases}
			\xi & \text{if } z \in \overline{D} \cup \bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}} L_n, \\
			\Phi_n(z) & \text{if } z \in \overline{W'_n} \text{ for some }n, \\
			0 & \text{if } z \in K_n \text{ for some }n,
		\end{cases}
	\end{equation*}
	which is continuous on $E$ and holomorphic on the interior of $E$, and the piecewise constant function $\varepsilon \colon E \to (0,\infty)$,
	\begin{equation*}
		\varepsilon(z) \coloneqq \begin{cases}
			\min\set*{\frac{r_{\xi}}{2}, \frac{1}{2}} & \text{if } z \in \overline{D} \cup \bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}} L_n \cup \bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}} K_n, \\
			\min\set{\varepsilon_n, R_{n+1}, \rho_{n+1}} & \text{if } z \in \overline{W'_n} \text{ for some }n.\\
		\end{cases}
	\end{equation*}
	By \Cref{thm:nersesjan}, there exists an entire function $f$ such that
	\begin{equation*}
		\abs{f(z) - \Psi(z)} < \varepsilon(z)
	\end{equation*}
	for all $z \in E$.
	
	This function $f$ satisfies conditions~\textit{\ref{prop:theorem-D-1}}--\textit{\ref{prop:theorem-D-6}} of \Cref{thm:theorem-D}.
	Indeed, conditions~\textit{\ref{prop:theorem-D-1}} and \textit{\ref{prop:theorem-D-2}} follow directly from our choices for the pertinent sets. 
    Condition~\textit{\ref{prop:theorem-D-3}} follows from our choice of $\varepsilon_n$ since we have
	\begin{equation*}
		f(\gamma_n) \subset f(\overline{V_n}) \subset \overline{D(\zeta_{n+1}, 2\rho_{n+1})} \subsetneq V_{n+1} = \operatorname{int}\gamma_{n+1}.
	\end{equation*}
	Similarly, \textit{\ref{prop:theorem-D-4}}, and even more directly \textit{\ref{prop:theorem-D-5}}, follow by our choice of the error function. 
    Lastly, \textit{\ref{prop:theorem-D-6}} follows from our choice of $\beta_n$. 
    The function $f$ has to be transcendental since no nonconstant polynomial can satisfy \textit{\ref{prop:theorem-D-5}}, and $f$ is nonconstant by \textit{\ref{prop:theorem-D-3}}.
	
	Consequently, \Cref{thm:theorem-D} yields that $f$ has simply connected wandering domains $U_n$ such that 
	\begin{equation*}
		\overline{V_n} = \overline{\operatorname{int}\gamma_n} \subset U_n \subset \operatorname{int}\Gamma_n = W_n' \subset W_n.
	\end{equation*}
	Hence, property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-4} holds.
	Property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-1} follows directly from property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-1}.
	Furthermore, note that by construction, for all $z \in \overline{D(\zeta_n, 3 \rho_n)}$,
	\begin{equation*}
		\Phi_n(z) + g^n(w_n)^d h(z, g^n(w_n)) \in \widetilde{K}_{n+1},
	\end{equation*}
	and 
	\begin{equation*}
		\abs{\Phi_n(z) - f(z)} < R_{n+1}.
	\end{equation*} 
	Together with the definition of $K_{n+1}$, property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-5} follows.

    From \ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-4}, it follows that, for $n \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$, $j > n$, and $z \in \overline{D(\zeta_n, 3\rho_n)}$,
    \begin{equation*}
        \begin{split}
            f(z) + g^{n}(w_j)^d h(z, g^{n}(w_j)) &\in f(\overline{V_n}) + g^{n}(w_j)^d h(z, g^{n}(w_j)) \\
             
            &\subset \overline{D(\zeta_{n+1}, 2\rho_{n+1})} + D(0,\rho_{n+1}) \\
             
            &\subset \overline{D(\zeta_{n+1}, 3\rho_{n+1})}.
        \end{split}
    \end{equation*}
    Hence, property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-6} holds.
    Moreover, observe that by \ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-8}, for all $n \in \mathbb{N}_{0}$ and $z \in K_{n+1}$,
    \begin{equation*}
        \begin{split}
            \abs{f(z) + g^{n+1}(w_n)^d h(z,g^{n+1}(w_n))} &\leq\abs{f(z)} + \abs{g^{n}(w_n)}^d\sup\abs{h(\overline{W_{n+1}}, \overline{\mathbb{D}})} < \frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{2} = 1,
        \end{split}
    \end{equation*}
    which is property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-7}.
    Lastly, property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-1-extra} follows from \ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-2}.
    This completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{lemma:tame-perturbation-2}
		There exist sequences $(\zeta_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of complex numbers, $(\rho_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of positive real numbers, and $(w_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}$ of nonzero complex numbers such that for all $n \geq 0$,
		\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
			\item $\abs{w_n} < \min\set*{\delta_g, \frac{1}{n+1}}$, \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-1}
			\item $\overline{D(\zeta_n, 3\rho_n)} \subset V_n$,\label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-2}
			\item $\overline{D(\zeta_{n+1}, 3\rho_{n+1})} + g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\overline{D(\zeta_n, 3 \rho_n)}, g^{n}(w_n)) \subset \paren*{W_{n+1} \setminus \overline{V_{n+1}}} \cap \mathbb H_{n+1}$, for an open half-plane $\mathbb H_{n+1}$ with $\mathbb H_{n+1} \cap \overline{V_{n+1}} = \emptyset$,\label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-3}
			\item $\abs{g^{k}(w_n)}^d \max_{z \in \overline{D(\zeta_k, 3\rho_k)}, w \in \overline{\mathbb{D}}} \abs{h(z,w)} < \rho_{k+1}$ for all $k \in \set{0,\dots, n-1}$, \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-4}
			\item $h(\overline{D(\zeta_n, 3\rho_n)}, \overline{D(0,\abs{g^n(w_n)})}) \subset \mathbb{H}\paren*{\frac{h(\zeta_n, 0)}{2},h(\zeta_n, 0)}$, \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-5}  
			\item $h(\zeta_n, 0) \neq 0$, \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-6}  
			\item $\abs{g^n(w_n)}^d \sup \abs{h(\overline{D(\zeta_n, 3\rho_n)}, \overline{D(0,\abs{g^n(w_n)})})} < \operatorname{dist}(\partial V_{n+1}, \partial W_{n+1})$, and \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-7}  
			\item $ \abs{g^n(w_n)}^d \sup \abs{h(\overline{W_{n+1}}, \overline{\mathbb{D}})} < \frac{1}{2} $. \label{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-8} 
		\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
The sequences are constructed inductively.

		Choose any $\zeta_0 \in V_0$ such that $h(\zeta_0, 0) \neq 0$; hence, property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-6} holds. 
        Such an element exists since $z \mapsto h(z, 0)$ is not identically zero. 
        By continuity, there exist $\rho_0 > 0$ and $w_0 \in \mathbb{C}\setminus\set{0}$ such that $h(\overline{D(\zeta_0, 3\rho_0)}, \overline{D(0,\abs{w_0})}) \subset \mathbb{H}(\frac{h(\zeta_0, 0)}{2},h(\zeta_0, 0))$, i.e., property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-5} holds. 
        Further shrinking $\rho_0$ guarantees that $\overline{D(\zeta_0, 3\rho_0)} \subset V_0$, yielding property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-2}, and further shrinking $w_0$, we obtain properties~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-1}, \ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-7}, and \ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-8}.

        Suppose $\zeta_0, \dots, \zeta_n$, $\rho_0, \dots, \rho_n$, and $w_0, \dots, w_n$ have been constructed satisfying properties~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-1} through \ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-8}.
        We construct the next elements as follows.

		Using property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-5}, we first obtain
		\begin{equation*}
			g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\overline{D(\zeta_n, 3 \rho_n)}, g^{n}(w_n)) \subset \mathbb{H}\paren*{g^{n}(w_n)^d\frac{h(\zeta_n, 0)}{2},g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\zeta_n, 0)}.
		\end{equation*}
		Applying \Cref{lemma:outside-jordan} to the Jordan domain $V_{n+1}$ with $v \coloneqq g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\zeta_n, 0) \neq 0$ yields a point $x \in \partial V_{n+1}$ such that 
		\begin{equation*}
			\overline{V_{n+1}} \cap \mathbb{H}\paren*{x, g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\zeta_n, 0)} = \emptyset.
		\end{equation*}
        Set $\mathbb{H}_{n+1} \coloneqq \mathbb{H}\paren*{x, g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\zeta_n, 0)}$.
		Then, for any $z \in V_{n+1} \cap D\paren*{x, \abs*{\frac{g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\zeta_n, 0)}{4}}} \neq \emptyset$, we obtain
		\begin{equation*}
			z + g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\overline{D(\zeta_n, 3 \rho_n)}, g^{n}(w_n)) \subset \paren*{\mathbb{C} \setminus \overline{V_{n+1}}} \cap \mathbb H_{n+1}.
		\end{equation*}
		Choose any $\zeta_{n+1} \in V_{n+1} \cap D\paren*{x, \abs*{\frac{g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\zeta_n, 0)}{4}}}$ with $h(\zeta_{n+1}, 0) \neq 0$.
		Thus, property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-6} holds.
		Furthermore, let $R > 0$ be small enough so that
		\begin{equation*}
			\overline{D(\zeta_{n+1}, 3R)} \subset D\paren*{x, \abs*{\frac{g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\zeta_n, 0)}{4}}}.
		\end{equation*}
		We now show that property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-3} holds for all $\rho_{n+1} \leq R$. We have already proven that
		\begin{equation*}
			\overline{D(\zeta_{n+1}, 3\rho_{n+1})} + g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\overline{D(\zeta_n, 3 \rho_n)}, g^{n}(w_n)) \subset  \paren*{\mathbb{C} \setminus \overline{V_{n+1}}} \cap \mathbb H_{n+1}.
		\end{equation*}
		Using $\overline{D(\zeta_{n+1}, 3\rho_{n+1})} \subset V_{n+1}$ and property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-7} shows that
		\begin{equation*}
			\overline{D(\zeta_{n+1}, 3\rho_{n+1})} + g^{n}(w_n)^d h(\overline{D(\zeta_n, 3 \rho_n)}, g^{n}(w_n)) \subset W_{n+1}.
		\end{equation*}
		Taken together, these inclusions yield property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-3}.
		Lastly, we construct $\rho_{n+1}$ and $w_{n+1}$. By our choice of $\zeta_{n+1}$, we have $h(\zeta_{n+1},0)\neq 0$. By continuity, there exist $w_{n+1} \in \mathbb{C}\setminus\set{0}$ and $\rho_{n+1} > 0$ so small that
		\begin{equation*}
			h(\overline{D(\zeta_{n+1}, 3\rho_{n+1})}, \overline{D(0,\abs{g^{n+1}(w_{n+1})})}) \subset \mathbb{H}\paren*{\frac{h(\zeta_{n+1}, 0)}{2},h(\zeta_{n+1}, 0)}.
		\end{equation*}
		Without loss of generality, we may assume that $\rho_{n+1} \leq R$, yielding properties~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-3} and \ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-5}.
		Further shrinking $\rho_{n+1}$ yields property~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-2}, and further shrinking $w_{n+1}$ yields properties~\ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-1}, \ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-4}, \ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-7}, and \ref{prop:tame-perturbation-lemma-2-8}.
